- **Walkthrough**: constants `HeadersAttribute = "mmca-headers"`, `DeadLetterReasonHeader`, `DeadLetterDetailHeader` and `MaxDeadLetterTextLength = 4096` (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:22,25,28,31`). `PackHeaders` writes the pairs as a JSON object (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:38-55`); `UnpackHeaders` keeps only string-valued properties, returns empty for non-object text and clears on a `JsonException` (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:64-94`). `ToPublishRequest` builds the SNS publish with the UTF-8 body and the packed attribute (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:100-114`). `ToHeaders` takes any other string attribute under its own name, then overlays the unpacked `mmca-headers`, so the framework's own headers win (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:123-152`). `ToIncoming` builds the [`IncomingBrokerMessage`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#incomingbrokermessage) from body, headers and `MessageId` (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:158-167`). `RedeliveryHeaders` copies the headers and sets `BrokerHeaders.RedeliveryCount` (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:176-184`). `DeadLetterHeaders` adds the reason and, when present, the detail (removing a stale detail otherwise), each cut to 4096 characters (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:195-213,249-250`). `ToSendRequest` builds an SQS send with the body, `DelaySeconds` and the packed attribute (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:221-237`). `ToDelaySeconds` rounds the delay up to whole seconds and clamps it to 0 to 900 (`AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:246-247`).
- **Why it's built this way**: packing all headers into one attribute removes the 10-attribute ceiling entirely, and keeping the mapping static and side-effect free lets the wire format be pinned by unit tests.
- **Where it's used**: [`AmazonSqsPublisher`](#amazonsqspublisher) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsPublisher.cs:36`), [`AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler`](#amazonsqsdeliveryhandler) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler.cs:67,75,104-105,113`) and [`QueueSettlement`](#queuesettlement) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:279,282`); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Messaging/AmazonSqsMessageMappingTests.cs`.

### AmazonSqsPublisher
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsPublisher.cs:12` · Level 6 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the SNS implementation of [`IBrokerPublisher`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerpublisher): one publish per outgoing message to the event's topic.
- **Depends on**: [`IBrokerPublisher`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerpublisher), [`OutgoingBrokerMessage`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#outgoingbrokermessage), [`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner`](#amazonsqstopologyprovisioner), [`AmazonSqsEntityNames`](#amazonsqsentitynames), [`AmazonSqsMessageMapping`](#amazonsqsmessagemapping); AWS SDK `IAmazonSimpleNotificationService`.
- **Concept**: fan-out by topic. A publisher only knows topics; each subscribing host's queue receives a copy through its SNS subscription. See [`AzureServiceBusPublisher`](#azureservicebuspublisher) for the Azure sibling.
- **Walkthrough**: the constructor null-checks the SNS client and the provisioner (`AmazonSqsPublisher.cs:20-27`). `PublishAsync` (`AmazonSqsPublisher.cs:30`) resolves the topic ARN through `GetTopicArnAsync` on the fitted topic name (`AmazonSqsPublisher.cs:34`), then publishes the mapped request (`AmazonSqsPublisher.cs:36`).
- **Why it's built this way**: delegating topic resolution to the provisioner means a missing topic is created on the first publish when `MessageBus:ProvisionTopology` is on, and fails with a named error when it is off (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:69-78`); the ARN is cached after the first lookup.
- **Where it's used**: registered as the `IBrokerPublisher` singleton by [`AmazonSqsTransport`](#amazonsqstransport)`.Register` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransport.cs:96-98`); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Messaging/AmazonSqsTopologyTests.cs` and `AmazonSqsTransportTests.cs`.

### AmazonSqsReceiver
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:20` · Level 6 · class (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the hosted `BackgroundService` that provisions the host's queues, long-polls its SQS queue and runs each message through [`AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler`](#amazonsqsdeliveryhandler) with bounded concurrency.
- **Depends on**: [`BrokerTopology`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertopology), [`MessageBusSettings`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#messagebussettings), [`BrokerTransportPolicies`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertransportpolicies), [`AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler`](#amazonsqsdeliveryhandler), [`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner`](#amazonsqstopologyprovisioner), [`QueueSettlement`](#queuesettlement); AWS SDK `IAmazonSQS`; BCL `BackgroundService`, `SemaphoreSlim`.
- **Concept**: a hand-written pull consumer. SQS has no push processor, so the receive loop, backpressure and shutdown drain live here; compare the Azure processor-based [`AzureServiceBusReceiver`](#azureservicebusreceiver). `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses throughput controls and backpressure; a semaphore of `ConcurrentMessageLimit` slots caps in-flight work and each receive asks only for as many messages as there are free slots. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses diagnosable failure; every failure path logs through a source-generated `[LoggerMessage]` (EventIds 20 to 23, `AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:260-270`).
- **Walkthrough**: constants `DefaultConcurrentMessageLimit = 16`, `MaxMessagesPerReceive = 10` and `WaitTimeSeconds = 20` (the SQS long-poll maximum) (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:23,26,29`); backoff runs from 2 seconds to 1 minute (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:31-32`). `ResolveConcurrency` reads `MessageBus:ConcurrentMessageLimit`, else 16 (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:74-75`); `ResolveBatchSize` reads `MessageBus:PrefetchCount`, else 10, clamped to 1 to 10 (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:80-81`). `BuildReceiveRequest` asks for every message attribute with the 60-second visibility timeout (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:87-94`). `ExecuteAsync` returns at once when the host has no subscriptions, provisions with retry, then runs the loop and, in `finally`, drains by acquiring every slot (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:97-128`). `ReceiveLoopAsync` waits for one slot, takes any others free right now with a zero timeout, receives up to that many, returns unused slots, and starts one `Task.Run` per message (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:130-189`); a receive failure releases the slots, logs and backs off (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:164-176`). `ProcessAsync` calls the handler, logs a settle failure and always releases its slot (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:191-207`). `ProvisionWithRetryAsync` retries [`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner`](#amazonsqstopologyprovisioner)`.ProvisionAsync` with doubling backoff until it succeeds or the host stops (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:214-243,245`).
- **Why it's built this way**: neither provisioning nor a receive failure may crash the host, so both log and back off (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:209-213`), and the drain on stop guarantees every in-flight handler has released its message before the service returns (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:121-126`).
- **Where it's used**: registered with `AddHostedService` by [`AmazonSqsTransport`](#amazonsqstransport)`.Register` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransport.cs:100-109`); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Messaging/AmazonSqsTopologyTests.cs` and `AmazonSqsTransportTests.cs`.

### AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:24` · Level 6 · class (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: creates (or, with provisioning off, only looks up) the SNS topics, the host's SQS queue and error queue, the queue policy and the raw-delivery subscriptions.
- **Depends on**: [`BrokerTopology`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertopology), [`AmazonSqsEntityNames`](#amazonsqsentitynames), [`AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler`](#amazonsqsdeliveryhandler) (for the visibility timeout); AWS SDK `IAmazonSQS`, `IAmazonSimpleNotificationService`; BCL `ConcurrentDictionary`, `Utf8JsonWriter`.
- **Concept**: idempotent infrastructure-as-startup, the SQS counterpart of [`AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner`](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner). `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses least privilege; the queue policy grants `sqs:SendMessage` to the SNS service principal only when `aws:SourceArn` equals one of the subscribed topics, and to nothing else (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:144-180`). `[Rubric §17, DevOps]` assesses environment setup without manual steps; with `MessageBus:ProvisionTopology` on, a fresh account gets its whole topology on first start.
- **Walkthrough**: `ErrorQueueRetentionSeconds = 1_209_600` (14 days, the SQS maximum) (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:27`); topic ARNs are cached in a `ConcurrentDictionary` (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:33`). `GetTopicArnAsync` returns a cached ARN, else creates the topic when provisioning or finds it otherwise, throwing a message that names `MessageBus:ProvisionTopology` when it is missing (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:59-81`). `ProvisionAsync` (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:92`) with provisioning off only looks up the two queue URLs (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:99-104`); otherwise it ensures the error queue and the queue, reads the queue ARN, resolves every distinct consumed topic, sets the queue policy, then subscribes the queue to each topic (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:106-131`) and logs EventId 1 (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:133,270`). `QueueAttributes` sets the 60-second visibility timeout and `ErrorQueueAttributes` the 14-day retention (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:187-190,194-197`). `EnsureQueueAsync` falls back to `GetQueueUrl` on `QueueNameExistsException`, leaving a queue created elsewhere as it is (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:203-214`). `EnsureRawSubscriptionAsync` subscribes with `RawMessageDelivery = true`; on `InvalidParameterException` it finds the existing subscription by paging `ListSubscriptionsByTopic` and switches it to raw delivery (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:220-245,247-268`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the policy is set before the subscriptions so no message a new subscription delivers is refused (`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner.cs:83-88`), and raw delivery keeps the SNS envelope off the body so the receiver sees exactly what the publisher sent. Fail-fast when a required entity is missing follows [ADR-070](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/070-fail-fast-configuration-contract.html).
- **Where it's used**: a singleton built by [`AmazonSqsTransport`](#amazonsqstransport)`.Register` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransport.cs:89-94`), consumed by [`AmazonSqsPublisher`](#amazonsqspublisher) and [`AmazonSqsReceiver`](#amazonsqsreceiver); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Messaging/AmazonSqsTopologyTests.cs`.

### AmazonSqsTransport
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransport.cs:40` · Level 6 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the Amazon SQS/SNS [`IBrokerTransport`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokertransport), selected by `MessageBus:Provider=AmazonSqs` (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:43`), built on the AWS SDK SQS and SNS clients alone (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:14-16`). It registers the connection, provisioner, publisher and receiver that make up the transport.
- **Depends on**: [`IBrokerTransport`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokertransport), [`BrokerTransportContext`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertransportcontext), [`MessageBusSettings`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#messagebussettings), [`IBrokerPublisher`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerpublisher), [`IBrokerReceivePipeline`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerreceivepipeline), [`AmazonSqsTransportSettings`](#amazonsqstransportsettings), [`AwsStaticCredentials`](#awsstaticcredentials), [`AmazonSqsConnection`](#amazonsqsconnection), [`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner`](#amazonsqstopologyprovisioner), [`AmazonSqsPublisher`](#amazonsqspublisher), [`AmazonSqsReceiver`](#amazonsqsreceiver), [`AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler`](#amazonsqsdeliveryhandler); AWS SDK `RegionEndpoint`, `FallbackRegionFactory`.
- **Concept**: a pluggable transport strategy; cross-reference [`AzureServiceBusTransport`](#azureservicebustransport) for the shared shape. Topology is an SNS topic per event, the host's SQS queue subscribed to each consumed topic with raw delivery, a queue policy limited to those topics, and a `{queue}-error` queue (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:18-24`). `[Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration]` assesses broker portability and poison-message handling; this transport implements the neutral contract and adapts redelivery to the SQS delay cap. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` also applies: the vendor transport lives only in this adapter package, and the cloud-vendor lists in [`ArchitectureRules`](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#architecturerules) forbid the `Amazon` namespace root, `AWSSDK.*` assemblies and `MMCA.Common.Aws` from cloud-neutral assemblies (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Layering/ArchitectureRules.CloudVendors.cs:34,41,55,62`).
- **Walkthrough**: constants `ProviderName = "AmazonSqs"` and `MaxDelaySeconds = 900` (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:43,46`). The public constructor falls back to `FallbackRegionFactory.GetRegionEndpoint` (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:51-54`); an internal one accepts a test fallback (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:58-59`). `Name` returns the provider name and `MaxRedeliveryDelay` declares 900 seconds (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:62,65`). `Register` (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:71`) reads the settings, resolves the region, the static credentials and an absolute `ServiceUrl` before any service is built, so a configuration error fails while `AddBrokerMessaging` runs (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:76-84`). It then adds `TimeProvider.System` if absent, the [`AmazonSqsConnection`](#amazonsqsconnection) singleton, the [`AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner`](#amazonsqstopologyprovisioner) singleton with `settings.ProvisionTopology`, the [`AmazonSqsPublisher`](#amazonsqspublisher) as `IBrokerPublisher`, and the [`AmazonSqsReceiver`](#amazonsqsreceiver) hosted service around a new [`AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler`](#amazonsqsdeliveryhandler) (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:86-109`). `ReadSettings` binds `MessageBus:AmazonSqs`, else defaults (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:115-121`). `ResolveRegion(IConfiguration)` delegates to the static overload (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:130-131`), which uses the configured system name, else the fallback, else throws naming the key and `AWS_REGION` (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:141-153`). `LoggerFor<T>` falls back to `NullLoggerFactory` when no logger factory is registered (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:155-156`).
- **Why it's built this way**: SQS caps a message delay at 15 minutes, so the transport declares that cap as `MaxRedeliveryDelay` and the framework's receive pipeline shortens every second-level redelivery interval to it (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:27-30`). Every header travels packed in one message attribute, so the SQS/SNS 10-attribute cap never applies (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:24-25`). All configuration is validated in `Register` (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:32-37`). See [ADR-066](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/066-broker-transport-selection.html), [ADR-087](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/087-broker-poison-message-handling.html), [ADR-118](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/118-message-transport-library-policy.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered by `AddAmazonSqsTransport` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:86-87`); `MaxDelaySeconds` bounds [`AmazonSqsMessageMapping`](#amazonsqsmessagemapping)`.ToDelaySeconds` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsMessageMapping.cs:246-247`); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Messaging/AmazonSqsTransportTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the vendor lists also keep entries for the retired MassTransit SQS transport (`ArchitectureRules.CloudVendors.cs:39,60`), described there as retired vendor transports (`ArchitectureRules.CloudVendors.cs:48`); this transport itself is built on the AWS SDK clients alone (`AmazonSqsTransport.cs:14-16`).

### QueueSettlement
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:273` · Level 6 · class (private sealed, nested in [`AmazonSqsReceiver`](#amazonsqsreceiver))

- **What it is**: the production [`IAmazonSqsSettlement`](#iamazonsqssettlement), bound to one received message's receipt handle and the host's queue and error-queue URLs through a primary constructor (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:273`).
- **Depends on**: [`IAmazonSqsSettlement`](#iamazonsqssettlement), [`AmazonSqsMessageMapping`](#amazonsqsmessagemapping); AWS SDK `IAmazonSQS`.
- **Concept**: the adapter end of the settlement port introduced by [`IAmazonSqsSettlement`](#iamazonsqssettlement); the Azure counterpart is [`ProcessorSettlement`](#processorsettlement).
- **Walkthrough**: `DeleteAsync` calls `DeleteMessage` with the receipt handle (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:275-276`). `SendToQueueAsync` sends `ToSendRequest` to the queue with the requested delay (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:278-279`). `SendToErrorQueueAsync` sends it to the error queue with `delaySeconds: 0` (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:281-282`). `ChangeVisibilityAsync` calls `ChangeMessageVisibility` with the receipt handle (`AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:284-285`).
- **Why it's built this way**: SQS addresses a received message only by its receipt handle, so one small instance per message carries that handle and keeps the handler free of SDK calls.
- **Where it's used**: created once per received message in `ReceiveLoopAsync` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Messaging/AmazonSqsReceiver.cs:185`); no use outside the defining file.

### RdsIamAuthenticationSettings
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Rds` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Rds/RdsIamAuthenticationSettings.cs:8` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the bound options for RDS IAM database authentication, section `Rds:IamAuthentication` (`RdsIamAuthenticationSettings.cs:11`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator`](#rdsiamauthenticationconfigurator), which introduces token-as-password authentication.
- **Walkthrough**: `Enabled` (`RdsIamAuthenticationSettings.cs:14`) is the gate, off when absent. `Region` (`RdsIamAuthenticationSettings.cs:21`) is the database's region, blank deferring to the SDK chain. `AccessKeyId` / `SecretAccessKey` (`RdsIamAuthenticationSettings.cs:28,31`) follow the [`AwsStaticCredentials`](#awsstaticcredentials) both-or-neither rule.
- **Why it's built this way**: an explicit boolean gate (rather than presence of a key) lets a deployment switch PostgreSQL onto IAM tokens by configuration alone; see [ADR-113](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/113-postgresql-as-a-first-class-engine.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: bound in `AddAwsRdsIamAuthentication` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:112`); `SectionPath` is quoted in the TLS error of [`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator`](#rdsiamauthenticationconfigurator) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Rds/RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:116`).

### SnsInstallation
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsInstallation.cs:12` · Level 0 · record (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the in-memory shape of one row of the DynamoDB installation registry: `InstallationId`, `UserId`, `Platform`, `PushChannel` and `EndpointArn` (`SnsInstallation.cs:12-17`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`SnsInstallationRegistry`](#snsinstallationregistry), which explains why SNS needs a side table at all (SNS keeps no owner per endpoint).
- **Walkthrough**: a positional record of five non-nullable strings. `UserId` holds the invariant string form produced by `SnsInstallationRegistry.UserKey` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:49-50`), not the typed identifier. `Platform` is the normalized value (`fcmv1` or `apns`), which is what lets [`SnsNativePushSender`](#snsnativepushsender) attribute each endpoint to Android or Apple when it counts an outcome.
- **Where it's used**: materialized by `SnsInstallationRegistry.FromItem` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:204-220`), written by `TryPutAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:74`), built by [`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar`](#snspushdeviceregistrar) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:187`), and grouped by endpoint in [`SnsNativePushSender`](#snsnativepushsender) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSender.cs:65`).

### AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Secrets` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:27` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the `ConfigurationProvider` that reads every AWS Secrets Manager secret under one name prefix into `IConfiguration`, mapping names the same way the Key Vault provider does so one secret-name convention serves both clouds: the prefix is stripped and `--` becomes the configuration separator, so `adc/prod/ConnectionStrings--Default` arrives as `ConnectionStrings:Default` (`AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:8-12`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party at runtime; it is built by [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationsource). Externals: `Amazon.SecretsManager` (`IAmazonSecretsManager`, `ListSecretsRequest`, `BatchGetSecretValueRequest`), `Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration` (`ConfigurationProvider`, `ConfigurationPath`), and BCL `PeriodicTimer` / `CancellationTokenSource` (`AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:1-3`).
- **Concept introduced, secrets as a configuration source with a fail-loud first load and a fail-soft reload.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether secrets live outside code and deployment artifacts and reach the process through an identity-scoped channel: here they are read from Secrets Manager at startup, and the error a missing grant raises names the exact IAM actions to grant (`:183-188`). `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether a dependency failure degrades rather than takes down a running system: the first load must succeed (a secret that cannot be read fails startup, naming it, `:20-22`), but a failed timed reload keeps the values already loaded and records the exception in `LastReloadFailure` instead of blanking settings the host is using (`:124-129`). `[Rubric §33, Developer Experience]` applies through the shared naming convention: a team moving between Azure and AWS keeps one secret-name scheme.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Constants and state.** `BatchSize = 20` is the most ids one `BatchGetSecretValue` call accepts (`:30`); `KeyDelimiter` is `"--"` (`:32`). The client, prefix, optional reload interval, a disposal `CancellationTokenSource` and the polling task are the fields (`:34-38`). The constructor null-guards the client and prefix (`:44-49`).
  - **`KeyFor(secretName, namePrefix)`** (`:61-69`) returns `null` when the name is outside the prefix (an ordinal `StartsWith`) or equals the prefix exactly, otherwise the suffix with every `--` replaced by `ConfigurationPath.KeyDelimiter`.
  - **`Load()`** (`:72-86`) blocks once on `LoadAsync`, under a scoped `VSTHRD002` suppression (`:78-80`). The comment explains why the wait is safe: `ConfigurationProvider.Load` is synchronous by contract, the AWS SDK v4 client is async only, and no synchronization context is held while the configuration manager adds a source (`:74-77`). When a reload interval is set and no poller exists yet, it starts `PollAsync` (`:82-85`).
  - **`LoadAsync`** (`:132-143`) lists names, then reads them in chunks of `BatchSize` into a `Dictionary` keyed with `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` (`:135`, `:137`).
  - **`ListNamesAsync`** (`:145-172`) pages `ListSecrets` with a `Name` filter on the prefix (`:154`) and re-checks every name through `KeyFor` (`:161`). The re-check matters because the service filter is a case-insensitive prefix match, so a secret for another environment in a different case would otherwise slip in (`:13-17`).
  - **`ReadBatchAsync`** (`:174-202`) pages `BatchGetSecretValue`. Any per-secret error throws an `InvalidOperationException` that lists each secret id, error code and message and states the fix: grant `secretsmanager:GetSecretValue` (and `kms:Decrypt` on a customer-managed key) or move the secret out of the prefix (`:183-188`). Only `SecretString` values are kept, so a binary-only secret is skipped (`:191-196`).
  - **`PollAsync` / `ReloadOnceAsync`** (`:96-110`, `:112-130`) tick a `PeriodicTimer`; each tick reloads, clears `LastReloadFailure`, and only when `HasSameData` (`:204-206`) reports a difference swaps `Data` and calls `OnReload()`, so an unchanged secret set fires no change token. Any non-cancellation exception is stored, not thrown (`:124-129`).
  - **`Dispose()`** (`:89-94`) cancels the poller and disposes the token source and the client the provider owns.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-061](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/061-runtime-secret-management.html) records runtime secret management, and mirroring the Key Vault provider's name mapping and reload posture is what keeps the AWS adapter a drop-in sibling rather than a second convention. Re-checking the prefix ordinally and comparing whole data sets before `OnReload` keep the provider from importing a wrong-environment secret or churning every `IOptionsMonitor` on each tick.
- **Where it's used**: constructed only by [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationsource)`.Build` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource.cs:26-27`), which [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationExtensions](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationextensions) adds. Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProviderTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: no host in `MMCA.ADC/Source` calls `AddAwsSecretsManagerConfiguration`, so which deployments read secrets this way is Not determinable from source here.

---

### S3FileStorageSettings
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3FileStorageSettings.cs:13` · Level 0 · class (public sealed)

- **What it is**: the options class bound from the `FileStorage` section for the S3-compatible adapter, one shape that targets AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, MinIO, LocalStack or the GCS XML API (`S3FileStorageSettings.cs:13-73`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party. Externals: `Amazon.Runtime` (`RequestChecksumCalculation`, `ResponseChecksumValidation`).
- **Concept introduced, one client, many S3-compatible vendors.** `[Rubric §32, Dependency & Supply-Chain]` assesses whether a vendor dependency is isolated and its defaults are understood rather than inherited: both checksum properties default to `WHEN_REQUIRED` instead of the AWS SDK v4 default `WHEN_SUPPORTED`, because the v4 SDK attaches CRC32 trailers and `x-amz-checksum-*` headers that R2, older MinIO releases and the GCS XML API reject (`:58-72`). `[Rubric §31, Cost & FinOps]` is served by the same portability: the bucket vendor is a configuration value.
- **Walkthrough**: `SectionName` is `"FileStorage"` (`:16`). `Bucket` is required and provisioned by infrastructure (`:19`). `Region` is the AWS region, or the signing region when `ServiceUrl` is set (R2 expects `auto`), and falls back to the SDK chain when blank (`:21-26`). `ServiceUrl` selects an S3-compatible endpoint (`:28-33`); `ForcePathStyle` switches to `{endpoint}/{bucket}/{key}` addressing, needed by MinIO and LocalStack (`:35-39`). `PublicBaseUrl` overrides the returned URL, for a CDN or R2 public domain (`:41-46`). `AccessKeyId` / `SecretAccessKey` are an optional static pair; setting only one fails registration (`:48-56`). `RequestChecksumCalculation` and `ResponseChecksumValidation` default to `WHEN_REQUIRED` (`:65`, `:72`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html) keeps the core cloud-neutral with vendor adapters beside it, and [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html) records the storage contract. Sharing the `FileStorage` section name with [AzureBlobFileStorageSettings](#azureblobfilestoragesettings) means a host switches vendor by its registration call alone.
- **Where it's used**: bound by `AddS3FileStorage` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:55-58`), which registers nothing when `Bucket` is blank (`:58-61`) and resolves the key pair at registration (`:64`). Read by [S3StorageClientFactory](#s3storageclientfactory), [S3ObjectUrl](#s3objecturl) and [S3FileStorageService](#s3filestorageservice); tested across `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/`.

---

### SnsNativePushSettings
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSettings.cs:12` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the bound options for SNS mobile push, section `NativePush:Sns` (`SnsNativePushSettings.cs:15`), gated by the shared sibling key `NativePush:Enabled` (`SnsNativePushSettings.cs:18`).
- **Depends on**: [`DeviceInstallationRequest`](group-10-notifications.md#deviceinstallationrequest) for the normalized platform constants.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`AwsServiceCollectionExtensions`](#awsservicecollectionextensions) (`AddAmazonSnsPush`) for the gate-plus-completeness registration pattern this class feeds.
- **Walkthrough**: `FcmPlatformApplicationArn` and `ApnsPlatformApplicationArn` (`SnsNativePushSettings.cs:21,24`) are the SNS platform applications; `InstallationsTable` (`SnsNativePushSettings.cs:27`) is the DynamoDB registry; `Region`, `ServiceUrl`, `AccessKeyId`, `SecretAccessKey` (`SnsNativePushSettings.cs:33,36,42,45`) follow the package's shared endpoint and credential rules. There is no topic setting: every send targets endpoints read from the registry. `IsComplete` (`SnsNativePushSettings.cs:51-53`) requires the table and at least one platform application. `PlatformApplicationArnFor(platform)` (`SnsNativePushSettings.cs:58-67`) maps `DeviceInstallationRequest.FcmV1Platform` / `ApnsPlatform` to the configured ARN, returning `null` for an unknown platform or a blank ARN.
- **Why it's built this way**: allowing one platform application lets a deployment run one platform only (the registrar comment cites an Android-first app with no APNs application, `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:60`). The completeness check is what turns an incomplete section into a registration no-op rather than a runtime failure ([ADR-070](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/070-fail-fast-configuration-contract.html)).
- **Where it's used**: `AddAmazonSnsPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:178-190`), [`SnsPushClientFactory`](#snspushclientfactory), and through `IOptions<SnsNativePushSettings>` in [`SnsInstallationRegistry`](#snsinstallationregistry) (the table name) and [`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar`](#snspushdeviceregistrar) (the platform application per platform, `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:57`).

### SnsPushMessage
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushMessage.cs:12` · Level 1 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: builds the JSON-structured SNS publish body that carries one platform-specific payload per protocol.
- **Depends on**: [`NativePushPayloads`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#nativepushpayloads) for the FCM v1 and APNs payloads; `System.Text.Json`.
- **Concept**: SNS "message structure json": with `MessageStructure = "json"` (`SnsPushMessage.cs:15`), SNS reads a dictionary of per-protocol entries and delivers each subscriber its own entry. Reusing the core payload builders keeps the device-side shape identical across the Azure, GCP and AWS push adapters. `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]` assesses whether shared logic is reused across adapters; here the vendor adapter only wraps the core payloads.
- **Walkthrough**: `Build(title, body, metadata)` (`SnsPushMessage.cs:27`) computes the FCM v1 and APNs payloads (`SnsPushMessage.cs:29-30`) and serializes four entries (`SnsPushMessage.cs:32-38`): `default` (the body text), `GCM` (the FCM payload wrapped in the `fcmV1Message` envelope), `APNS` and `APNS_SANDBOX` (the same APNs payload for both).
- **Why it's built this way**: the doc comment records that SNS rejects a JSON-structured publish without a `default` entry, and that `default` is what a non-platform subscriber (an operator-added e-mail or SQS subscription) receives (`SnsPushMessage.cs:18-21`). Duplicating APNs into both keys means one message serves a production or sandbox platform application. See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: [`SnsNativePushSender`](#snsnativepushsender) for both targeted and broadcast publishes (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSender.cs:42,77-78,94`).

### AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Secrets` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Secrets/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource.cs:14` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the `IConfigurationSource` that carries a client factory, a name prefix and an optional reload interval, and builds one [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationprovider) from them (`AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource.cs:14-28`).
- **Depends on**: [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationprovider); externals `IAmazonSecretsManager`, `IConfigurationSource`.
- **Concept**: the standard source/provider split of `Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration`. `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether a dependency can be substituted: taking a `Func<IAmazonSecretsManager>` rather than a client lets a test hand in a fake, and gives each `Build` a fresh client that the provider owns and disposes.
- **Walkthrough**: a primary constructor over `clientFactory`, `namePrefix` and `reloadInterval` (`:14-17`); `NamePrefix` (`:20`) and `ReloadInterval` (`:23`) are exposed internally so tests can assert what the extension configured; `Build` calls the factory and constructs the provider (`:26-27`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the factory defers client construction to the moment configuration is built, after the extension has resolved credentials and endpoint.
- **Where it's used**: added by [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationExtensions](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationextensions)`.AddAwsSecretsManagerConfiguration` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationExtensions.cs:127-130`) only when `SecretsManager:NamePrefix` is set (`:85-94`), with a static key pair when one is configured (`:97-98`), and a `ReloadIntervalMinutes` that must parse as a positive whole number or the call throws (`:103-115`). Asserted in `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationExtensionsTests.cs`.

### S3ObjectUrl
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3ObjectUrl.cs:9` · Level 1 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: the pure function that composes the absolute URL an S3 upload returns, from the settings, the client's resolved region and the object key (`S3ObjectUrl.cs:9-69`).
- **Depends on**: [S3FileStorageSettings](#s3filestoragesettings); BCL `Uri`, `UriBuilder`.
- **Concept**: the S3 API returns no object URL on `PutObject`, so the adapter derives one. `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether logic with many branches is isolated where it can be tested without I/O: this is a static function with no client, covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/S3ObjectUrlTests.cs`.
- **Walkthrough**: `Compose` (`:29-63`) escapes the key first (`:34`), then takes the first branch that applies. A `PublicBaseUrl` that is absolute wins: `{PublicBaseUrl}/{key}`, without repeating the bucket (`:36-39`). An absolute `ServiceUrl` gives `{ServiceUrl}/{bucket}/{key}` when `ForcePathStyle` is set (`:45-48`), otherwise a `UriBuilder` prepends the bucket to the host (`:50-51`). Otherwise AWS S3 in the resolved region, path-style or virtual-hosted (`:60-62`); with no region known it throws an `InvalidOperationException` naming the three settings that would fix it (`:54-58`). `EscapeKey` splits on `/`, applies `Uri.EscapeDataString` to each segment and rejoins, so separators survive and spaces or non-ASCII characters do not break the URL (`:65-66`). `TrimEnd` drops a trailing slash from a base (`:68`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the doc orders the branches explicitly (`:11-23`) because each vendor addresses objects differently; a CDN or R2 public domain already points at the bucket, so repeating it would produce a broken link.
- **Where it's used**: [S3FileStorageService](#s3filestorageservice)`.UploadAsync` composes the URL before uploading (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3FileStorageService.cs:46`).

### S3StorageClientFactory
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3StorageClientFactory.cs:10` · Level 1 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: maps [S3FileStorageSettings](#s3filestoragesettings) onto an `AmazonS3Config` and creates the `IAmazonS3` client, with static credentials or the AWS default chain (`S3StorageClientFactory.cs:10-54`).
- **Depends on**: [S3FileStorageSettings](#s3filestoragesettings), [AwsClientEndpoint](#awsclientendpoint), [AwsStaticCredentials](#awsstaticcredentials); externals `Amazon.S3` (`AmazonS3Config`, `AmazonS3Client`).
- **Concept**: endpoint and credential selection shared with the other AWS adapters through [AwsClientEndpoint](#awsclientendpoint) and [AwsStaticCredentials](#awsstaticcredentials), so S3, Secrets Manager and SNS resolve regions and keys the same way. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether production uses an identity rather than a stored key: with no key pair configured the client falls back to the default credential chain (an IAM role in production).
- **Walkthrough**: `SectionPath = "FileStorage"` names the section in configuration errors (`:13`). `BuildConfig` (`:23-37`) copies `ForcePathStyle` and both checksum modes (`:27-32`), then lets `AwsClientEndpoint.Apply` choose between a service URL with a signing region and a plain region (`:34`). `Create` (`:45-53`) resolves credentials (`:48`) and constructs `AmazonS3Client` with or without them (`:50-52`).
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the mapping in a static factory makes the config testable without a network call (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/S3StorageClientFactoryTests.cs`). [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html) and [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html) cover the adapter and the storage contract.
- **Where it's used**: `AddS3FileStorage` resolves the key pair against `SectionPath` at registration, so a half-configured pair fails there rather than on the first upload, then registers `Create` as the `IAmazonS3` singleton with `TryAddSingleton`, so a client the host registered first wins (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:65-67`).

### RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Rds` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Rds/RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:20` · Level 2 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: an [`INpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inpgsqldatasourceconfigurator) that replaces the PostgreSQL password with a short-lived RDS IAM token signed locally for each connection string's own host, port and user.
- **Depends on**: [`INpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inpgsqldatasourceconfigurator), [`ConnectionStringSettings`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#connectionstringsettings), [`DataSourcesSettings`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#datasourcessettings), [`RdsIamAuthenticationSettings`](#rdsiamauthenticationsettings); Npgsql `NpgsqlDataSourceBuilder.UsePeriodicPasswordProvider`, AWS SDK `RDSAuthTokenGenerator`.
- **Concept introduced, token-as-password database auth.** Instead of a stored password, the data source asks a periodic provider for a SigV4-signed token with a 15-minute lifetime (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:12-15`). Because the token is a bearer credential sent as the password, the configurator refuses any connection string that does not require TLS. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses credential handling; here no database password exists to leak, and the transport is forced encrypted. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` is also relevant: it plugs into the core data-source configurator, so every context and the readiness check share one data source per connection string (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:92-94`).
- **Walkthrough**: primary-constructor parameters `region` and `credentials`, both nullable (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:20`). `TokenRefreshInterval` is 10 minutes (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:24`) and `FailureRetryInterval` 30 seconds (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:27`). `Configure(builder)` (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:31`) checks TLS on the builder's connection string (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:35-37`) and installs `UsePeriodicPasswordProvider(GenerateTokenAsync, ...)` (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:39`). `EnsureConfiguredConnectionStringsAreEncrypted(configuration)` (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:50`) checks the top-level `ConnectionStrings:PostgreSQLConnectionString` and every `DataSources:{name}:PostgreSQLConnectionString` already in configuration (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:54-59`), parsing each through `NpgsqlDataSourceBuilder` so it sees what `Configure` will see (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:65-66`). `GenerateTokenAsync` (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:75`) requires a `Host` and `Username` (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:82-86`), then picks one of four `RDSAuthTokenGenerator.GenerateAuthTokenAsync` overloads by which of credentials and region are set (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:90-105`). `EnsureEncrypted` (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:108`) accepts `Require`, `VerifyCA` or `VerifyFull` (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:110`) and otherwise throws a message naming the source and the fix (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:115-118`).
- **Why it's built this way**: refreshing every 10 minutes keeps the current token well inside its 15-minute lifetime (`RdsIamAuthenticationConfigurator.cs:23`). Checking configured connection strings at registration makes a cleartext misconfiguration fail at startup, while `Configure` still catches a connection string supplied later ([ADR-070](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/070-fail-fast-configuration-contract.html)). See [ADR-113](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/113-postgresql-as-a-first-class-engine.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: constructed and registered by `AddAwsRdsIamAuthentication` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:125,140`); exercised by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Rds/AwsRdsIamAuthenticationTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: SQL Server, SQLite and Cosmos sources are untouched (`AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:99-100`), since this only implements the Npgsql configurator.

### SnsPushClientFactory
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushClientFactory.cs:12` · Level 2 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: builds the SNS and DynamoDB clients the SNS push adapter needs, from one [`SnsNativePushSettings`](#snsnativepushsettings) and optional static credentials.
- **Depends on**: [`SnsNativePushSettings`](#snsnativepushsettings), [`AwsClientEndpoint`](#awsclientendpoint); AWS SDK SNS and DynamoDB clients.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`AwsClientEndpoint`](#awsclientendpoint); the same factory shape as [`S3StorageClientFactory`](#s3storageclientfactory).
- **Walkthrough**: `CreateSns` (`SnsPushClientFactory.cs:18`) and `CreateDynamoDb` (`SnsPushClientFactory.cs:34`) are symmetric: a fresh config, `AwsClientEndpoint.Apply(config, settings.ServiceUrl, settings.Region)` (`SnsPushClientFactory.cs:23,39`), then the client with or without `credentials` (`SnsPushClientFactory.cs:25-27,41-43`). One `ServiceUrl` therefore redirects both services to the same emulator.
- **Where it's used**: the `TryAddSingleton` factories in `AddAmazonSnsPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:191-192`), so a host-registered client wins.

### SnsInstallationRegistry
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:20` · Level 2 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the DynamoDB-backed table of push installations (one item per installation id) plus the SNS endpoint-removal helper, used because SNS keeps no owner per endpoint.
- **Depends on**: [`SnsInstallation`](#snsinstallation), [`SnsNativePushSettings`](#snsnativepushsettings); AWS SDK `IAmazonDynamoDB` and `IAmazonSimpleNotificationService`.
- **Concept introduced, ownership rules as conditional writes.** The [`IPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar) contract says an installation id may be claimed when it is new, already the caller's, or another user's whose current push channel the caller presents (the same device changing hands). Rather than read-then-write, the rule is one DynamoDB `ConditionExpression`, `OwnershipCondition` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:36`), so a race between two writers cannot both win. `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses consistency under concurrency; here the guarantee is atomic at the store. `[Rubric §11, Security]` applies too: a user cannot hijack another device's installation.
- **Walkthrough**: `UserIdIndexName = "UserId-index"` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:29`) names the GSI used for user-targeted sends; it must project at least `EndpointArn` and `Platform` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:26-27`), since the sender reads both from each row. `UserKey` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:49-50`) is the invariant-culture string form of a user id. `GetAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:56`) reads with `ConsistentRead = true` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:63`) because ownership decisions follow. `TryPutAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:74`) writes the five attributes (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:76-83`) under the ownership condition (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:92`) and returns `false` on `ConditionalCheckFailedException` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:102-105`). `QueryByUserAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:112`) pages the GSI until `LastEvaluatedKey` is empty (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:140-145`). `DeleteOwnedAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:157`) and `DeleteIfEndpointAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:168`) are conditional deletes through `DeleteWhenAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:178`), which swallows a failed condition because the row has changed hands or endpoints (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:195-198`). `RemoveEndpointAsync` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:175-176`) is a single `DeleteEndpoint` call, idempotent on the SNS side. `FromItem` (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:204`) returns `null` for a row missing the id, user or endpoint (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:209-213`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the conditional deletes make cleanup safe against interleaving: an owner delete leaves a row that was handed over (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:150-151`), and a stale-endpoint cleanup leaves a row refreshed onto a new endpoint (`SnsInstallationRegistry.cs:161-162`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered as a singleton in `AddAmazonSnsPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:193`); consumed by [`SnsNativePushSender`](#snsnativepushsender) and [`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar`](#snspushdeviceregistrar).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the table and its `UserId-index` GSI are provisioned outside the application (`AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:161-163`); nothing here creates or validates them.

### EndpointTally
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSender.cs:158` · Level 2 · class (private sealed, nested in `SnsNativePushSender`)

- **What it is**: the per-platform counter one [`SnsNativePushSender`](#snsnativepushsender) send accumulates and then turns into a [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome) measured in endpoints (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:154-158`).
- **Depends on**: [`DeviceInstallationRequest`](group-10-notifications.md#deviceinstallationrequest) for the `fcmv1` / `apns` constants, [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome) and [`NativePushDeliveryUnit`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushdeliveryunit).
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome) for the shared outcome contract; this class is only the SNS adapter's way of filling it in.
- **Walkthrough**: four private counters (Android sent and failed, Apple sent and failed) and the first failure seen (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:160-164`). `Delivered` sums the two sent counters and feeds the "published to N endpoints" log line (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:166`). `Fail(exception)` keeps only the first failure (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:168`). `Count(platform, failure)` records any failure, then increments the sent or failed counter for `DeviceInstallationRequest.FcmV1Platform` (Android) or `ApnsPlatform` (Apple) by ordinal comparison (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:170-199`); a platform that is neither is not counted, though its failure still is (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:155-156`). `ToOutcome()` calls `NativePushOutcome.From` with `NativePushDeliveryUnit.Endpoint` and the five values (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:201-202`).
- **Why it's built this way**: SNS delivers per platform endpoint, not per installation, so the outcome unit is the endpoint, and the platform is read from the endpoint's rows because every row of one endpoint group registered the same token on the same platform (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:69-71`). A failed user lookup goes through `Fail` alone, so the send reports a failure without inventing a platform count for devices it never saw (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:60-61`).
- **Where it's used**: only inside `SnsNativePushSender.SendToUsersAsync` (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:47,61,71,75-76`); no usage outside the defining file.

### SnsNativePushSender
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsNativePushSender.cs:36` · Level 3 · class (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the SNS implementation of [`INativePushSender`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inativepushsender): a per-user send publishes to each of the users' endpoints and returns a [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome) counted in endpoints per platform. There is no broadcast path and no topic.
- **Depends on**: [`INativePushSender`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inativepushsender), [`SnsInstallationRegistry`](#snsinstallationregistry), [`SnsInstallation`](#snsinstallation), [`SnsPushMessage`](#snspushmessage), [`EndpointTally`](#endpointtally), [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome); AWS SDK SNS client, `LoggerMessage` source generation. The constructor takes the registry, the SNS client and a logger, no settings (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:36-39`).
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`SnsInstallationRegistry`](#snsinstallationregistry). `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses partial-failure behavior; here a lookup failure skips only that user and a publish failure skips only that endpoint, and both still surface in the returned outcome rather than vanishing into a log.
- **Walkthrough**: `SendToUsersAsync` (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:42`) builds the message once (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:46`) and a fresh [`EndpointTally`](#endpointtally) (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:47`), de-duplicates user keys (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:49`), and queries each user's installations; a service or transport failure is logged, recorded with `tally.Fail`, and the loop continues (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:54-63`). Rows are grouped by `EndpointArn` so two installation ids sharing one endpoint get one publish (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:65`), and each publish result is counted against the group's platform (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:67-71`). The method logs `tally.Delivered` and returns `tally.ToOutcome()` (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:75-76`). `TryPublishAsync` (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:81`) targets the endpoint (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:88`) and returns `null` on success (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:93`); `EndpointDisabledException` or `NotFoundException` triggers stale cleanup and is returned as the failure (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:95-99`), and any other service or transport failure is logged and returned (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:100-104`). `RemoveStaleAsync` (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:111`) removes the endpoint once and then every row pointing at it with the endpoint-conditional delete (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:116-120`), logging rather than throwing on failure (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:122-125`). Five source-generated log methods follow (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:128-141`). `IsTransportFailure` (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:150-152`) treats `HttpRequestException`, `IOException`, `TimeoutException` and `OperationCanceledException` as transport failures only while the caller's token is not cancelled, the same predicate as [`S3FileStorageService`](#s3filestorageservice) (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:143-149`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the AWS SDK lets connect, DNS, dropped-connection and timeout failures escape unwrapped, so they are handled like a service failure, while the caller's own cancellation always propagates (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:143-149`). A disabled or missing endpoint counts as failed because its device was not reached; the class doc notes that a bad APNs credential shows up this way (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:26-31`). Removing every row for a stale endpoint means no surviving row fails its own publish later (`SnsNativePushSender.cs:107-110`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered transient as `INativePushSender` by `AddAmazonSnsPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:194`), replacing [`NullNativePushSender`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#nullnativepushsender); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Notifications/SnsNativePushSenderTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: how callers act on the returned outcome is outside this file.

### SnsPushDeviceRegistrar
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:32` · Level 4 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the SNS implementation of [`IPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar): it maps a device installation onto an SNS platform endpoint, records ownership in the DynamoDB registry, and removes a user's endpoints and rows on request.
- **Depends on**: [`IPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar), [`DeviceInstallationRequest`](group-10-notifications.md#deviceinstallationrequest), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [`SnsInstallationRegistry`](#snsinstallationregistry), [`SnsInstallation`](#snsinstallation), [`SnsNativePushSettings`](#snsnativepushsettings); AWS SDK SNS client.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`SnsInstallationRegistry`](#snsinstallationregistry) for the ownership rule, and [`AzureNotificationHubDeviceRegistrar`](#azurenotificationhubdeviceregistrar), whose rule this one states it mirrors (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:163-164`). Errors come back as [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) failures, not exceptions. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses compensation on partial failure; the registrar orders and undoes SNS side effects so no endpoint is left without a row to account for it.
- **Walkthrough**: `UpsertAsync` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:39`) normalizes `fcmv1` / `apns` case-insensitively (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:43-48`), returns `PushDevice.UnsupportedPlatform` validation failures for an unknown platform (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:49-55`) or one without a configured platform application (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:57-65`), and maps a service or transport failure to `PushDevice.UpsertFailed` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:71-77`). `RegisterAsync` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:173`) reads the row, returns `PushDevice.InstallationConflict` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:156-160`) when `ClaimedByAnotherDevice` is true (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:168-171,180-183`), ensures an endpoint (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:185`), and writes the row with `TryPutAsync` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:187-188`). A refused write removes the endpoint only when this request created it (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:195-198`); a row that moved endpoints has its old endpoint removed (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:203-208`). `EnsureEndpointAsync` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:218`) reuses a same-platform endpoint via `TryRefreshEndpointAsync`, which sets `Token` and `Enabled = "true"` and returns `false` on `NotFoundException` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:239-260`); otherwise it calls `CreatePlatformEndpoint` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:228-231`). `DeleteAsync` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:82`) returns success for an unknown or foreign id (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:88-93`), removes the endpoint only when no other row of the same user shares it (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:99-106`), then deletes the row conditionally on the owner (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:108`); failures map to `PushDevice.DeleteFailed` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:111-118`). `DeleteAllForUserAsync` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:128`) lists the owner's rows through the `UserId` index (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:133`), removes each distinct endpoint (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:134-137`), then deletes every row conditionally on the owner (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:139-142`); failures also map to `PushDevice.DeleteFailed` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:146-153`). `RemoveUnaccountedAsync` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:263`) is best-effort and only logs on failure (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:269-273`). Every catch filters on `AmazonServiceException` or `IsTransportFailure` (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:283-285`), the same predicate as [`S3FileStorageService`](#s3filestorageservice): `HttpRequestException`, `IOException`, `TimeoutException` or `OperationCanceledException`, and only while the caller's token is not cancelled.
- **Why it's built this way**: the conflict check runs before anything is created so the other device's endpoint and row stay untouched, and the conditional write repeats it atomically for a row that appears in between (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:177-179`). Delete runs endpoint first, row last, so a partial failure leaves a row the client's retry can find (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:95-98`); `DeleteAllForUserAsync` keeps the same order for the same reason, and a row handed over in the meantime survives its owner-conditional delete (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:122-127`). Reporting success for a foreign id avoids an existence oracle (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:90-91`). `CreatePlatformEndpoint` is idempotent per token, so a retried registration does not multiply endpoints (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:215-216`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered transient as `IPushDeviceRegistrar` by `AddAmazonSnsPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:195`), replacing [`NullPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#nullpushdeviceregistrar); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Notifications/SnsPushDeviceRegistrarTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the source names a known edge: when the conditional write is refused and `CreatePlatformEndpoint` had returned an existing endpoint for a token registered under another installation id, the undo removes that endpoint too; the other row's next send finds it gone and drops it, and its device re-registers on start (`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:192-194`).

### S3FileStorageService
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Storage/S3FileStorageService.cs:23` · Level 4 · class (public sealed)

- **What it is**: the S3-compatible implementation of [IFileStorageService](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ifilestorageservice): AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, MinIO and the GCS XML API through one client, against the single bucket in `FileStorage:Bucket`, with the same contract as [AzureBlobFileStorageService](#azureblobfilestorageservice) (`S3FileStorageService.cs:11-23`).
- **Depends on**: `IAmazonS3`, `IOptions<S3FileStorageSettings>` and `ILogger<T>` as primary-constructor parameters (`:23-26`); [S3FileStorageSettings](#s3filestoragesettings), [S3ObjectUrl](#s3objecturl), [FileUploadOptions](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#fileuploadoptions), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) / [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error).
- **Concept**: the same provider-neutral storage contract introduced at [AzureBlobFileStorageService](#azureblobfilestorageservice). `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether a write cannot leave inconsistent state behind: the object URL is composed before the upload, so a configuration that cannot name the object fails before any orphan is written (`:44-46`). `[Rubric §32, Dependency & Supply-Chain]` applies through the vendor-specific signing choice in `SendsUnsignedPayload`.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `_settings` unwraps the options with a null guard (`:28`); `IsConfigured` is `true` (`:31`). The two `UploadAsync` overloads carry the same `RS0026` suppressions as the Azure sibling (`:34`, `:39`).
  - `UploadAsync(..., options, ct)` (`:40-95`) composes the URL with the client's resolved region (`:46`), builds a `PutObjectRequest` with `AutoCloseStream = false` because the caller owns the stream (`:48-57`), sets `DisablePayloadSigning` when `SendsUnsignedPayload` is true (`:59-62`), and copies non-empty `ContentDisposition` and `CacheControl` headers (`:64-72`). Success returns the composed URI (`:76-77`); `AmazonServiceException` and transport failures both yield `FileStorage.UploadFailed` (`:79-94`).
  - `DeleteAsync` (`:98-125`) calls `DeleteObjectAsync`; the comment notes S3 delete is already idempotent (a missing key answers 204), which matches the contract (`:102-106`). Failures yield `FileStorage.DeleteFailed` (`:109-124`).
  - `SendsUnsignedPayload` (`:137-139`) is true only for an absolute HTTPS `ServiceUrl`. The doc gives the reason (`:127-134`): Cloudflare R2 does not implement streaming SigV4 chunked signing, TLS already protects the body, AWS S3 keeps the SDK default, and plain-HTTP endpoints (MinIO, LocalStack) must keep it because the SDK refuses an unsigned payload over HTTP.
  - `IsTransportFailure` (`:147-149`) answers `false` once the caller's token is cancelled, so the caller's own cancellation always propagates; otherwise `HttpRequestException`, `IOException`, `TimeoutException` and `OperationCanceledException` count as transport failures (a timeout after the client's own retries surfaces as either of the last two, `:141-146`). The SNS adapters reuse this predicate; the Azure sibling's is narrower, with no `TimeoutException` case (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Storage/AzureBlobFileStorageService.cs:107-109`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html) keeps the S3 SDK in a vendor assembly so the core stays cloud-neutral; [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html) and [ADR-123](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/123-speaker-session-assets.html) define the storage contract it honours. One S3-compatible adapter covering several vendors avoids a near-duplicate adapter per bucket provider.
- **Where it's used**: registered by `AddS3FileStorage` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:68`) when `FileStorage:Bucket` is set. Tested in `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/Storage/S3FileStorageServiceTests.cs` and `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws.Tests/AwsServiceCollectionExtensionsTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: no host in `MMCA.ADC/Source` calls `AddS3FileStorage`, so which deployments use this adapter, and against which vendor, is Not determinable from source here.

---

### AwsTelemetrySettings
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/AwsTelemetrySettings.cs:9` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the `AwsTelemetry` configuration section that drives [AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions](#awstelemetryexporterextensions): the region gate, the CloudWatch Logs destination, optional endpoint overrides and an optional static key pair (`AwsTelemetrySettings.cs:9-33`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; external `IConfiguration`.
- **Concept**: a settings class read key by key rather than bound through the options pattern, because the exporter decides at registration time whether to register anything at all. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses whether telemetry export is configured deliberately per deployment: every value here is nullable, and a blank `Region` keeps the whole exporter off.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`SectionPath`** (`:12`): the constant `"AwsTelemetry"`.
  - **Properties** (`:15-33`): `Region` (the gate, `:15`), `LogGroup` and `LogStream` (set together, `:18`, `:21`), `TracesEndpoint` and `LogsEndpoint` (full URLs overriding the regional X-Ray and CloudWatch Logs OTLP endpoints, `:24`, `:27`), `AccessKeyId` and `SecretAccessKey` (set together, or neither for the default chain, `:30`, `:33`). All are `string?` with `init` setters.
  - **`Read`** (`:38-47`): builds an instance by indexing `IConfiguration` once per property through `Key`.
  - **`Key`** (`:52`): returns `"AwsTelemetry:{name}"`, so error messages and endpoint lookups name the exact key.
- **Why it's built this way**: the pairing rules are enforced by the consumer, not here: the exporter throws when only one of `LogGroup` / `LogStream` is set (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:212-219`) and hands the key pair to [AwsStaticCredentials](#awsstaticcredentials) (`AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:112-115`).
- **Where it's used**: read once by `AddAwsTelemetryExporter()` (`AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:101`), with `Key` reused for endpoint overrides (`AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:194`).

### AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService
> MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup · `MMCA.Common.Azure` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService.cs:13` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed partial, `IHostedService`)

- **What it is**: a hosted service that logs one Warning at startup when the DataProtection key ring is persisted to Azure Blob Storage without an at-rest Key Vault key (`AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService.cs:13-32`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; externals `IHostedService`, `ILogger<T>`, `[LoggerMessage]`.
- **Concept**: a non-fatal startup notice for an optional hardening step. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether security gaps are surfaced rather than silent: the missing at-rest key is not a failure (see [AzureDataProtectionExtensions](#azuredataprotectionextensions)) but it is named in the log on every start.
- **Walkthrough**: `KeyVaultKeyUriKey` is the constant `"DataProtection:KeyVaultKeyUri"` (`:17`), which [AzureDataProtectionExtensions](#azuredataprotectionextensions) also reads as its gate-2 key. `StartAsync` calls the source-generated `LogNoAtRestKey` and completes (`:20-24`); `StopAsync` does nothing (`:27`). The message is a `LogLevel.Warning` that names the configuration key (`:29-32`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the registering method adds it only when the key is blank AND the environment is not Development (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureDataProtectionExtensions.cs:95-99`), so a developer machine stays quiet. Recorded in [ADR-069](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/069-shared-data-protection-key-ring.html).
- **Where it's used**: only by `AddAzureDataProtection()` (`AzureDataProtectionExtensions.cs:90`, `:98`).

### AzureTokenCredentials
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureTokenCredentials.cs:12` · Level 0 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: one factory, `Create(string? managedIdentityClientId)`, that returns the `TokenCredential` the Azure adapter signs in with (`AzureTokenCredentials.cs:20-23`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; externals `TokenCredential`, `DefaultAzureCredential`, `DefaultAzureCredentialOptions`.
- **Concept**: pinning a user-assigned managed identity from configuration. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether services authenticate with identities rather than shared keys: a blank id yields a plain `DefaultAzureCredential` (system-assigned identity, or whatever `AZURE_CLIENT_ID` selects), a set id yields one with `ManagedIdentityClientId` set to the trimmed value (`:21-23`).
- **Walkthrough**: a single expression-bodied method (`:20-23`); the doc comment on the parameter states the blank-means-default rule (`:15-18`).
- **Why it's built this way**: two consumers need the same rule (Service Bus and Redis Entra sign-in), so it lives in one place.
- **Where it's used**: the default constructor of [AzureServiceBusTransport](#azureservicebustransport) passes `AzureTokenCredentials.Create` as its credential factory (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:47-49`), and `AddAzureRedisEntraAuthentication` builds its configurator with it (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:253`).

### CredentialFailure
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/AwsSigV4ExportHandler.cs:321` · Level 0 · enum (private, nested in [AwsSigV4ExportHandler](#awssigv4exporthandler))

- **What it is**: the three reasons [AwsSigV4ExportHandler](#awssigv4exporthandler) refuses to send: `None` (no credential found), `Threw` (resolving the credential threw), `SigningFailed` (the signer threw) (`AwsSigV4ExportHandler.cs:321-326`).
- **Depends on**: nothing.
- **Concept**: a private reason code; see [AwsSigV4ExportHandler](#awssigv4exporthandler) for the refuse-and-warn-once pattern it feeds.
- **Walkthrough**: `Describe` maps each member to the log text, with an `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` for any other value (`AwsSigV4ExportHandler.cs:244-255`). Each member has its own warn-once flag (`_warnedNoCredentials`, `_warnedCredentialFailure`, `_warnedSigningFailure`, `:54-58`).
- **Where it's used**: only inside the defining file (`AwsSigV4ExportHandler.cs:106`, `:112`, `:126`, `:147`, `:153`, `:170`).
