### IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:6` · Level 0 · interface (internal)

- **What it is**: the credential source the AWS telemetry signing handler asks for `AWSCredentials` on every export, with an async and a sync member (`IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:15`, `:25`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; external `AWSCredentials` (AWS SDK).
- **Concept**: a small internal port that lets the handler be tested with a fixed credential and lets production resolve the SDK default chain lazily. `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether external dependencies can be substituted: the two implementations are [StaticAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#staticawstelemetrycredentialsource) and [DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#defaultchainawstelemetrycredentialsource).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`GetAsync(CancellationToken)`** (`:15`): returns the credentials or `null`; the contract allows it to throw too, and the handler treats both as "no credentials" and sends nothing (`:8-12`).
  - **`Get(CancellationToken)`** (`:25`): the synchronous twin for the exporter's synchronous send path; it may block the calling thread (the OTLP exporter calls it on its own background thread) and never waits on a task (`:18-22`).
- **Where it's used**: chosen once in `AddAwsTelemetryExporter()` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:124-126`), then passed to every [AwsSigV4ExportHandler](#awssigv4exporthandler) (`AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:238`, `:252`) and to [AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup](#awstelemetrycredentialwarmup).

---

### AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup.cs:19` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed partial, `IHostedService`)

- **What it is**: a hosted service that resolves the AWS default credential chain once while the host starts, so the first telemetry export already finds a credential (`AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup.cs:19-50`).
- **Depends on**: [IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#iawstelemetrycredentialsource); externals `IHostedService`, `SuppressInstrumentationScope` (OpenTelemetry), `[LoggerMessage]`.
- **Concept**: a warm-up that is a latency optimization, never a gate. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses whether the telemetry pipeline stays out of its own data and degrades visibly: the lookup runs inside `SuppressInstrumentationScope.Begin()` (`:28`) so the chain's own HTTP calls (container or instance metadata, STS) are not traced and exported as noise (`:26-27`), and every failure is a Warning, never a startup failure.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`StartAsync`** (`:24-47`): awaits `credentials.GetAsync` (`:31`); a `null` result logs `LogNoCredential` (`:32-35`). An `OperationCanceledException` while the host's token is cancelled is swallowed (`:37-40`); any other exception logs `LogWarmupFailed` with its type and message (`:42-46`, with `CA1031` suppressed and justified at `:41`).
  - **`StopAsync`** (`:50`): nothing.
  - **Messages**: event 3 `LogWarmupFailed` and event 4 `LogNoCredential`, both Warning, both stating that each export retries the default chain and sends nothing until one succeeds (`:52-62`).
- **Why it's built this way**: registered only when no static key pair is configured, as a singleton exposed through `AddHostedService` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:128-136`); the comment there states the chain is warmed at host start, never at registration, and that a failure never fails the host (`:128-129`). The retry on the next export is [DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#defaultchainawstelemetrycredentialsource) restarting a failed lookup. Recorded in [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: `AddAwsTelemetryExporter()` only (`AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:132-135`). No `MMCA.ADC/Source` host calls `AddAwsTelemetryExporter()` today.

---

### AzureDataProtectionExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureDataProtectionExtensions.cs:20` · Level 1 · class (static, `extension<TBuilder>`)

- **What it is**: `AddAzureDataProtection()`, which persists the ASP.NET Core DataProtection key ring to one Azure Blob and optionally encrypts it at rest with an Azure Key Vault key, so every replica of a scaled-out host shares one key ring (`AzureDataProtectionExtensions.cs:26-28`).
- **Depends on**: [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames) and [AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService](#azurekeyringatrestnoticeservice); externals `IHostApplicationBuilder`, `DefaultAzureCredential`, `AddDataProtection`, and the Azure `PersistKeysToAzureBlobStorage` / `ProtectKeysWithAzureKeyVault` extensions.
- **Concept**: the configuration-gated host integration with a startup receipt, taught at [AwsDataProtectionExtensions](#awsdataprotectionextensions). The receipt is registered only after the gate passes (`:71`), so a URI that appears only in a configuration source added after this call still trips the startup check (`:61-63`). `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether scale-out keeps sessions valid: a shared key ring is what lets cookies and antiforgery tokens minted by one replica decrypt on another.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Keys** (`:32-43`): `DataProtection:BlobStorageUri` (the gate), `DataProtection:ApplicationName` (defaults to the host application name), `DataProtection:KeyVaultKeyUri` (optional; outside Development its absence logs one Warning).
  - **Gate 1** (`:56-67`): blank URI returns the builder untouched, with no receipt, so developer machines, test hosts and the Helpdesk seed keep the in-memory default.
  - **Receipt** (`:69-71`): a `HostIntegrationMarker` named `AzureDataProtection`.
  - **Persistence** (`:73-81`): application name from configuration or the environment (`:73-74`), one `DefaultAzureCredential` shared by both sinks so they share a token cache (`:76-77`), then `SetApplicationName(...).PersistKeysToAzureBlobStorage(new Uri(blobStorageUri), credential)` (`:79-81`).
  - **Gate 2** (`:90-99`): reads `AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService.KeyVaultKeyUriKey` (`:90`); when set, `ProtectKeysWithAzureKeyVault` (`:91-94`); when blank and the environment is not Development, registers [AzureKeyRingAtRestNoticeService](#azurekeyringatrestnoticeservice) as a hosted service (`:95-99`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the comment at `:83-89` records the load-bearing decision: gate 2 is deliberately separate from gate 1 because the Key Vault Crypto User role is granted out of band and can lag the deployment; coupling them would turn an optional hardening gap into a total authentication outage, so a missing key is a Warning, not a failure (`:97`). Identity requirements (Storage Blob Data Contributor, plus Key Vault Crypto User when gate 2 is on) are stated at `:47-50`. Recorded in [ADR-069](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/069-shared-data-protection-key-ring.html), [ADR-122](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/122-dev-only-relaxations-fail-closed.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html); the GCP sibling is [GoogleCloudDataProtectionExtensions](#googleclouddataprotectionextensions).
- **Where it's used**: `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:106` and `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI.Web/Program.cs:47`. The tripwire message naming this call is at `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:111`.

### AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions.cs:23` · Level 1 · class (static, `extension<TBuilder>`)

- **What it is**: `AddAzureKeyVaultConfiguration()`, which adds an Azure Key Vault configuration source so every vault secret reads through `IConfiguration` and overrides the sources added before it (`AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions.cs:29-31`).
- **Depends on**: [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames); externals `AzureKeyVaultConfigurationOptions`, `AddAzureKeyVault`, `DefaultAzureCredential`.
- **Concept**: secrets as a configuration source, the Azure original of the convention [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationprovider) and [GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensions](#googlesecretmanagerconfigurationextensions) mirror. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether secrets live outside code and reach the process through an identity-scoped channel: authentication is `DefaultAzureCredential` (managed identity when deployed), needing Key Vault Secrets User on the vault (`:52-56`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Keys** (`:35-42`): `KeyVault:Uri` (the gate) and optional `KeyVault:ReloadIntervalMinutes`; unset means the vault is read once at startup.
  - **Naming** (`:45-49`): a double dash maps onto the configuration separator, so `ConnectionStrings--Default` arrives as `ConnectionStrings:Default`.
  - **Gate** (`:82-92`): a blank `KeyVault:Uri` returns the builder with no receipt, so a URI that only appears in a source added after this call still trips the startup check (`:84-88`).
  - **Reload interval** (`:94-111`): parsed with `InvariantCulture`; a non-positive or non-integer value throws `InvalidOperationException` (`:106-107`) rather than silently falling back to "never reload", per the comment at `:96-99`.
  - **Receipt** (`:113-115`): the `AzureKeyVaultConfiguration` marker, registered only after the reload interval has parsed.
  - **Source** (`:117`): `builder.Configuration.AddAzureKeyVault(new Uri(vaultUri), new DefaultAzureCredential(), options)`; an invalid URI surfaces as `UriFormatException` (documented at `:74-76`).
- **Why it's built this way**: deliberately not part of `AddServiceDefaults()`, because service defaults run in every host including developer machines and tests (`:59-63`). Because `ConfigurationManager` loads each source as it is added, the vault is read synchronously at this call, so it belongs early in `Program.cs` (`:66-70`). Recorded in [ADR-061](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/061-runtime-secret-management.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: every ADC host: `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:75`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI.Web/Program.cs:39`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:91`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:97`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:70` and `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:70`. The tripwire message naming this call is at `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:106`.

### AzureMonitorExporterExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Startup/AzureMonitorExporterExtensions.cs:12` · Level 1 · class (static, `extension<TBuilder>`)

- **What it is**: `AddAzureMonitorExporter()`, which exports logs, metrics and traces to Azure Monitor through the Azure Monitor OpenTelemetry distro when `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` is set (`AzureMonitorExporterExtensions.cs:24-28`).
- **Depends on**: [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames); externals `AddOpenTelemetry().UseAzureMonitor()`.
- **Concept**: an ordering precondition enforced in code. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses whether telemetry is exported reliably and filtered deliberately: the outbox-poll and probe filter processors registered by service defaults must run before this exporter's batch processor, otherwise the spans they un-record are exported anyway (`:30-33`).
- **Walkthrough**: `ConnectionStringKey` is the public constant `"APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING"` (`:18`). The method first scans the service collection for a non-keyed `HostIntegrationMarker` named `ServiceDefaultsTelemetry` and throws `InvalidOperationException` when it is missing (`:44-52`), regardless of the gate, so a misordered host fails on a developer machine (`:37-41`). It then returns when the connection string is blank, with no receipt (`:54-59`), and otherwise registers its own receipt (`:61-63`) and calls `UseAzureMonitor()` (`:65`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the gate keeps local runs free of an Azure dependency, and registering the receipt only after the gate means a connection string that appears in a source added later still trips the startup check (`:54-55`); the receipt lets the startup tripwire fail a host that has the connection string but not the call (`:61-62`). Both this exporter and the vendor-neutral OTLP exporter can be active at once, each shipping its own copy (`:32-33`). Recorded in [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html); the GCP sibling is [GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions](#googlecloudtelemetryexporterextensions) and the AWS sibling is [AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions](#awstelemetryexporterextensions).
- **Where it's used**: every ADC host, directly after service defaults: `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/MMCA.ADC.Gateway/Program.cs:82`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI.Web/Program.cs:46`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:98`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:118`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:75` and `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:85`. The tripwire message naming this call is at `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:101`.

### DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:19` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the [IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#iawstelemetrycredentialsource) used when no static key pair is configured: it resolves the AWS SDK default credential chain lazily, caches the first non-null result, and retries a failed lookup on the next call (`DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:19-116`).
- **Depends on**: [IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#iawstelemetrycredentialsource); externals `DefaultAWSCredentialsIdentityResolver`, `AWSCredentials`, `Lock`, `Volatile`.
- **Concept**: a single-flight, success-only cache. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether hot paths avoid repeated expensive work: once resolved, both members return the cached credential through a `Volatile.Read` with no lock (`:56-60`, `:80-84`); concurrent async callers share one in-flight lookup.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **State** (`:21-31`): the two resolver delegates, two locks (`_sync` for the async lookup, `_synchronousResolution` for the sync path), the cached `_resolved` and the in-flight `_lookup` task.
  - **Constructors**: the public one wires `DefaultAWSCredentialsIdentityResolver.GetCredentials` and `GetCredentialsAsync` with `clientConfig: null` (`:34-39`); the internal one takes any two resolvers, for tests (`:44-51`).
  - **`GetAsync`** (`:54-75`): under `_sync`, starts a new `ResolveAsync()` when there is no lookup or the previous one completed without caching a result (a failure, `:65-69`), then returns `lookup.WaitAsync(cancellationToken)` (`:74`), so one caller's cancellation ends only its own wait.
  - **`Get`** (`:78-104`): throws if already cancelled (`:86`), then takes `_synchronousResolution`, re-checks the cache because another exporter thread may have resolved it meanwhile (`:89-94`), calls the sync resolver (`:96`) and caches a non-null result (`:97-100`).
  - **`ResolveAsync`** (`:106-115`): awaits the async resolver and caches a non-null result.
- **Why it's built this way**: a `null` or a thrown lookup is never cached, so a host that starts before its IAM role is available recovers on a later export; [AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup](#awstelemetrycredentialwarmup) pre-warms the same instance at startup.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by `AddAwsTelemetryExporter()` when the static key pair is absent (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:124-125`).

---

### StaticAwsTelemetryCredentialSource
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/StaticAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:7` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed, primary constructor)

- **What it is**: the [IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#iawstelemetrycredentialsource) for a configured static key pair: both members return the `AWSCredentials` passed to its constructor (`StaticAwsTelemetryCredentialSource.cs:7-15`).
- **Depends on**: [IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#iawstelemetrycredentialsource); external `AWSCredentials`.
- **Concept**: the trivial sibling of [DefaultChainAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#defaultchainawstelemetrycredentialsource); see [IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#iawstelemetrycredentialsource) for the port.
- **Walkthrough**: `GetAsync` wraps the credential in `ValueTask.FromResult` (`:10-11`); `Get` returns it (`:14`). Neither ever returns `null` or throws.
- **Where it's used**: constructed by `AddAwsTelemetryExporter()` when [AwsStaticCredentials](#awsstaticcredentials) resolves `AwsTelemetry:AccessKeyId` and `AwsTelemetry:SecretAccessKey` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:112-115`, `:126`); with it, no [AwsTelemetryCredentialWarmup](#awstelemetrycredentialwarmup) is registered (`AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:130`).

### AwsSigV4ExportHandler
> MMCA.Common.Aws · `MMCA.Common.Aws.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/Telemetry/AwsSigV4ExportHandler.cs:30` · Level 2 · class (internal sealed partial, `DelegatingHandler`)

- **What it is**: the `DelegatingHandler` under each AWS OTLP exporter's `HttpClient` that signs every export request with AWS Signature Version 4 for X-Ray (`xray`) or CloudWatch Logs (`logs`), and refuses to send when it cannot sign (`AwsSigV4ExportHandler.cs:30-327`).
- **Depends on**: [IAwsTelemetryCredentialSource](#iawstelemetrycredentialsource), [CredentialFailure](#credentialfailure), [AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions](#awstelemetryexporterextensions) (for the log-destination header names, `:36-40`); externals `AWSSigV4Signer`, `AWSSigningRequest`, `AWSSigV4Parameters`, `RegionEndpoint`, `TimeProvider`, `[LoggerMessage]`.
- **Concept**: fail closed on authentication, warn once per failure kind. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether outbound calls are authenticated and never degrade to an unauthenticated form: when the credential is missing, its lookup throws, or signing throws, the handler returns a synthesized `401 Unauthorized` without calling the inner handler (`:179-180`), and the log text states that no unsigned request leaves the process (`:231-235`). `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` assesses whether failures are visible without flooding: each failure kind logs one Warning (event 1) for the handler's lifetime and Debug (event 2) after that, through `Interlocked.Exchange` on a per-kind flag (`:304-315`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Constants and fields** (`:33-58`): `content-type` is the one signed content header (`:33`); the CloudWatch Logs group and stream headers are signed when present (`:36-40`); three warn-once flags (`:54-58`).
  - **Constructor** (`:70-90`): guards every argument (`service` must be non-blank) and copies `addedHeaders`, the headers set on every request BEFORE signing (the logs exporter's `x-aws-log-group` / `x-aws-log-stream`, `:66-68`).
  - **`Send`** (`:93-133`): the synchronous path the exporter uses on its own background thread resolves the credential with `Get` (`:102`), refuses on a throw (`:104-108`) or `null` (`:110-114`), buffers the body (`:116`), signs with `AWSSigV4Signer.Sign` (`:122`), refuses on a signing failure (`:124-128`), applies the signature (`:130`) and sends (`:132`).
  - **`SendAsync`** (`:136-177`): the same steps with `GetAsync` (`:143`), `ReadAsByteArrayAsync` (`:157-160`) and `SignAsync` (`:164-166`).
  - **Body handling**: `ReadBody` (`:183-196`) and `ReplaceContent` (`:202-219`) swap the content for a `ByteArrayContent` copy with the same content headers (minus `Content-Length`), so the bytes on the wire are exactly the bytes hashed (`:198-201`).
  - **`PrepareSigningRequest`** (`:262-292`): adds the configured headers, then signs only `content-type` and the present log-destination headers; the signer adds `host` and its own `x-amz-*` headers (`:257-261`).
  - **`Parameters`** (`:294-301`): region, service, `SignPayload = true`, and `SignedAt` from the injected `TimeProvider`.
  - **`ApplySignature`** (`:221-229`): copies every signer header except `host` onto the request, replacing existing values.
  - **`Describe`** (`:244-255`): the reason text per [CredentialFailure](#credentialfailure), with the exception type and message appended (never key material, `:243`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the signed header set is fixed rather than "everything present", so headers the OTLP exporter or an intermediate handler adds cannot invalidate the signature (`:257-261`). A refreshing credential is resolved by the signer itself, so a failed refresh surfaces as a signing failure (`:120-121`). Recorded in [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html); the GCP counterpart is [GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler](#googlecloudtelemetryauthhandler).
- **Where it's used**: built inside the `HttpClientFactory` of each AWS exporter's `OtlpExporterOptions` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aws/AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:234-255`), with service `xray` or `logs` (`AwsTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:25`, `:28`).

---

### AzureServiceCollectionExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:31` · Level 7 · class (static, `extension(IServiceCollection)`)

- **What it is**: the Azure adapter's DI entry points: `AddAzureBlobFileStorage`, `AddAzureNotificationHubsPush`, `AddAzureServiceBusTransport`, `AddAzurePostgreSqlEntraAuthentication` and `AddAzureRedisEntraAuthentication` (`AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:44`, `:95`, `:154`, `:181`, `:230`).
- **Depends on**: [AzureBlobFileStorageSettings](#azureblobfilestoragesettings), [AzureBlobFileStorageService](#azureblobfilestorageservice), [AzureNotificationHubSettings](#azurenotificationhubsettings), [NotificationHubConnection](#notificationhubconnection), [INotificationHubRestClient](#inotificationhubrestclient), [NotificationHubRestClient](#notificationhubrestclient), [NotificationHubRetryPolicy](#notificationhubretrypolicy), [AzureNotificationHubNativePushSender](#azurenotificationhubnativepushsender), [AzureNotificationHubDeviceRegistrar](#azurenotificationhubdeviceregistrar), [AzureServiceBusTransport](#azureservicebustransport), [AzurePostgreSqlEntraAuthenticationSettings](#azurepostgresqlentraauthenticationsettings), [AzurePostgreSqlEntraAuthenticationConfigurator](#azurepostgresqlentraauthenticationconfigurator), [AzureRedisEntraAuthenticationSettings](#azureredisentraauthenticationsettings), [AzureRedisEntraAuthenticationConfigurator](#azureredisentraauthenticationconfigurator), [AzureTokenCredentials](#azuretokencredentials), [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames), [IFileStorageService](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ifilestorageservice), [INativePushSender](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inativepushsender), [IPushDeviceRegistrar](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar), [IBrokerTransport](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokertransport), [INpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inpgsqldatasourceconfigurator), [IRedisConfigurationOptionsConfigurator](group-09-caching.md#iredisconfigurationoptionsconfigurator); externals `BlobServiceClient`, `DefaultAzureCredential`, `IHttpClientFactory` and `ResilienceHandler`.
- **Concept**: config-gated, unconditionally-callable registrations through `extension(IServiceCollection)` members ([C# `extension(T)` types](00-primer.md#c-extensiont-types--read-this-once), [ADR-106](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/106-extension-members-as-public-di-surface.html)). `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` assesses whether infrastructure is swappable per deployment: each call is a no-op when its section is incomplete or disabled, so hosts call it always and deployments switch it on by configuration alone. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether data stores are reached with identities rather than shared secrets: the two Entra members put PostgreSQL and Redis on token login.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`AddAzureBlobFileStorage`** (`:44-74`): binds options (`:48-49`), returns when `ContainerName` is blank (`:52-55`) or when there is neither an absolute `ServiceUri` nor a connection string (`:58-62`; an empty-string `ServiceUri` binds as a relative `Uri`, `:57`). Registers a `BlobContainerClient` with `TryAddSingleton`, preferring `ServiceUri` + `DefaultAzureCredential` over the connection string (`:64-70`), then the transient service (`:71`).
  - **`AddAzureNotificationHubsPush`** (`:95-137`): binds options, requires `Enabled`, `ConnectionString` and `HubName` (`:103-108`), registers the parsed `NotificationHubConnection` via `TryAddSingleton` (`:110`) and the internal REST client as a typed `HttpClient` built with `NotificationHubRetryPolicy.Default` (`:111-116`). `RemoveAllLoggers()` drops the factory's per-request logging because hub URIs carry user tags and installation ids (`:117-120`), and a handler filter removes every `ResilienceHandler` (`:121-132`) since the client carries its own retry (three retries with exponential backoff or the hub's `Retry-After`) and a 60-second attempt timeout (`:87-90`). Then the internal sender and registrar (`:133-134`), replacing the core null pair ([ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html)).
  - **`AddAzureServiceBusTransport`** (`:154-155`): `services.AddBrokerTransport(new AzureServiceBusTransport())`; the doc comment says call it BEFORE `AddBrokerMessaging`, which selects the transport by `MessageBus:Provider=AzureServiceBus` ([ADR-066](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/066-broker-transport-selection.html)). With `MessageBus:AzureServiceBus:FullyQualifiedNamespace` set the bus signs in with an Entra token (optionally pinned by `ManagedIdentityClientId`) and ignores any connection string; otherwise the connection string resolves from `MessageBus:ConnectionString`, `ConnectionStrings:rabbitmq`, then `ConnectionStrings:messaging`; with neither, `AddBrokerMessaging` fails naming both (`:142-150`).
  - **`AddAzurePostgreSqlEntraAuthentication`** (`:181-208`): returns unless `AzurePostgreSql:EntraAuthentication:Enabled` is true (`:185-191`); fails registration for any configured PostgreSQL connection string that does not require TLS (`:193-195`); adds the `AzurePostgreSqlEntraAuthentication` receipt once (`:197-205`); registers an `AzurePostgreSqlEntraAuthenticationConfigurator` through `AddNpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator` (`:207`). The doc comment states the scope (`https://ossrdbms-aad.database.windows.net/.default`), the four-minute refresh and the no-password, TLS-required connection string contract (`:158-175`).
  - **`AddAzureRedisEntraAuthentication`** (`:230-254`): returns unless `AzureRedis:EntraAuthentication:Enabled` is true (`:234-240`); adds the `AzureRedisEntraAuthentication` receipt once (`:242-250`); registers an `AzureRedisEntraAuthenticationConfigurator` over `AzureTokenCredentials.Create(settings.ManagedIdentityClientId)` through `AddRedisConfigurationOptionsConfigurator` (`:252-253`). It reaches the Redis client, distributed cache, output-cache store, SignalR backplane and the health check's connection (`:211-213`); a connection string with a password or `ssl=false` fails when its connection is configured (`:223-225`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html), [ADR-070](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/070-fail-fast-configuration-contract.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html) place vendor code in adapters over core ports. The `TryAdd` client registration lets a host supply its own client first. The two Entra members check for an existing marker before adding one, so calling them twice registers one receipt, and the core startup tripwire fails a host that enables the gate without the call (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:151-152`, `:166-167`). The PostgreSQL TLS check runs at registration so an unencrypted connection string fails before the first query (`:193-194`); PostgreSQL as an engine is [ADR-113](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/113-postgresql-as-a-first-class-engine.html).
- **Where it's used**: blob storage at `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:225` and `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:301`; push at `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Notification/MMCA.ADC.Notification.API/DependencyInjection.cs:40` and `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:231`; Service Bus in every ADC service host (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:260`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:325`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:140`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:97`); Redis Entra in the same four (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:128`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:228`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:85`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:91`). No `MMCA.ADC/Source` host calls `AddAzurePostgreSqlEntraAuthentication`.

### AzureServiceBusEndpoint
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusEndpoint.cs:14` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the resolved answer to "where does the Service Bus transport connect": either a fully qualified namespace plus a Microsoft Entra credential, or a data-plane connection string plus the management-plane one (which, for the local emulator, points at a different port). It is computed and validated once while `AddBrokerMessaging` runs, and creating the clients from it performs no I/O (`AzureServiceBusEndpoint.cs:7-13`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party. Externals: `Azure.Core.TokenCredential`, `Azure.Messaging.ServiceBus.ServiceBusClient` and `ServiceBusAdministrationClient`. Built only by [AzureServiceBusTransport](#azureservicebustransport)`.ResolveEndpoint`.
- **Concept introduced, a connection description that cannot leak its secret.** `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses how secrets are kept out of logs and how a service authenticates: the type is deliberately a class and not a record, because a record's generated `ToString` would print a connection string's shared access key into any log line that formats it (`:11-12`), and the token path carries no key at all.
- **Walkthrough**: the constructor (`:25-37`) takes five values and stores them unchanged into five get-only properties: `FullyQualifiedNamespace` (`:40`), `Credential` (`:43`), `ConnectionString` (`:46`), `AdministrationConnectionString` (`:49`) and `IsEmulator` (`:52`). Exactly one of the two shapes is populated: the token shape sets the namespace and credential and leaves both strings null; the connection-string shape does the reverse. `CreateClient` (`:56-59`) and `CreateAdministrationClient` (`:63-66`) branch on `FullyQualifiedNamespace is not null`, so the namespace path always wins when present; on the connection-string path the data plane uses `ConnectionString` and the management plane uses `AdministrationConnectionString`. The caller owns the returned client.
- **Why it's built this way**: resolving the endpoint at registration time rather than on first use means a misconfigured host fails at startup with a message, not at its first publish ([AzureServiceBusTransport](#azureservicebustransport) `AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:74-76`). `IsEmulator` is the single flag the rest of the transport reads to decide whether entity quotas apply ([ServiceBusEmulatorSupport](#servicebusemulatorsupport)).
- **Where it's used**: wrapped by [AzureServiceBusConnection](#azureservicebusconnection) (`AzureServiceBusConnection.cs:15-22`); `IsEmulator` is passed to [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner) (`AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:87`). Exercised through `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusReceiverTests.cs`.

---

### AzureServiceBusTransportSettings
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransportSettings.cs:7` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the Service Bus transport's own configuration keys, bound from `MessageBus:AzureServiceBus` (`SectionName`, `AzureServiceBusTransportSettings.cs:10`): the namespace a host signs in to with a Microsoft Entra token instead of a shared access key.
- **Depends on**: nothing. The shared broker keys (connection string, emulator admin endpoint, provisioning, backpressure) live on [MessageBusSettings](group-14-module-system-composition.md#messagebussettings); this class carries only what is Azure-specific.
- **Concept introduced**: none new; it is the settings half of the managed-identity sign-in that [AzureServiceBusTransport](#azureservicebustransport) introduces. `[Rubric §11, Security]` applies: a namespace host name plus an identity client id is enough to connect, so the namespace can run with shared access keys switched off.
- **Walkthrough**: `FullyQualifiedNamespace` (`:17`) accepts a host name such as `contoso.servicebus.windows.net` or an `sb://` address; when set, the bus signs in with a token and any broker connection string is ignored (`:12-16`). `ManagedIdentityClientId` (`:24`) pins the user-assigned identity; blank selects the system-assigned identity (or the one `AZURE_CLIENT_ID` names), and it is read only alongside the namespace (`:19-23`). Both are `init`-only.
- **Where it's used**: bound by `AzureServiceBusTransport.ReadSettings` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:171-177`), with `SectionName` also composing the `FullyQualifiedNamespaceKey` error-message key (`:43`). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransportCredentialTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: which hosts set a namespace versus a connection string in each deployment is configuration, Not determinable from source.

### IAzureServiceBusSettlement
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/IAzureServiceBusSettlement.cs:10` · Level 0 · interface (internal)

- **What it is**: the four broker calls that settle one delivered Service Bus message, behind an interface so the disposition logic in [AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler](#azureservicebusdeliveryhandler) is unit-testable without a broker (`IAzureServiceBusSettlement.cs:5-9`). It is the Azure counterpart of [IAmazonSqsSettlement](#iamazonsqssettlement).
- **Depends on**: external `Azure.Messaging.ServiceBus.ServiceBusMessage` only.
- **Concept introduced**: `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether behavior can be tested in isolation: settling is the one part of a receive that needs a live lock token, so extracting just those calls lets every disposition branch be tested against a fake.
- **Walkthrough**: `CompleteAsync` (`:15`) removes the message; `DeadLetterAsync(reason, description, ...)` (`:22`) moves it to the queue's native dead-letter queue; `AbandonAsync` (`:27`) releases the lock so the broker delivers it again; `ScheduleOnQueueAsync(message, enqueueTime, ...)` (`:34`) schedules a new message on the host's own queue, which is how a second-level redelivery is expressed.
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [ProcessorSettlement](#processorsettlement) inside [AzureServiceBusReceiver](#azureservicebusreceiver); consumed by [AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler](#azureservicebusdeliveryhandler)`.HandleAsync`; faked in `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandlerTests.cs`.

### ServiceBusEmulatorSupport
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/ServiceBusEmulatorSupport.cs:26` · Level 0 · class (internal, static)

- **What it is**: the pieces the Azure Service Bus transport needs to run against the official local emulator instead of a real namespace, kept in one place so the production path reads only one flag, [AzureServiceBusEndpoint](#azureservicebusendpoint)`.IsEmulator`, set from the connection string (`ServiceBusEmulatorSupport.cs:6-9`). It answers two questions: is this connection string an emulator one, and if so, what is the management-plane connection string.

- **Depends on**: nothing first-party. External: `Azure.Messaging.ServiceBus.Administration` (named in the docs for `ServiceBusAdministrationClient`), BCL `Uri` and `CultureInfo` (`ServiceBusEmulatorSupport.cs:1-2`). Its input comes from [MessageBusSettings](group-14-module-system-composition.md#messagebussettings) (`ConnectionString` plus `EmulatorAdminEndpoint`).

- **Concept introduced, detection that keys off the artifact rather than the environment.** `[Rubric §17, DevOps]` assesses whether development and production run the same paths: the point of the emulator branch is that a local stack exercises the *same* transport production uses, instead of substituting RabbitMQ and discovering Service Bus specific behavior only after deployment. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses blast radius: the branch is entered only when the resolved connection string carries `UseDevelopmentEmulator=true`, a token a real namespace never emits, so no production deployment can reach any of this by accident. That is stated as the reason detection is not keyed off an environment name or a separate flag, either of which somebody can set in the wrong place (`ServiceBusEmulatorSupport.cs:10-16`). The emulator differs from the real service in two ways the type accounts for: it serves the data plane (AMQP) and the management plane (HTTP) on two ports, so the administration client needs its own connection string, and it enforces a one-hour ceiling on entity time-to-live and auto-delete, so every entity provisioned there must stay beneath it (`:17-24`).

- **Walkthrough**:
  - **The two constants.** `EmulatorMarker` is the literal `"UseDevelopmentEmulator=true"` (`ServiceBusEmulatorSupport.cs:31`). `EmulatorEntityQuota` is one hour, the ceiling the emulator enforces on entity time-to-live and auto-delete-on-idle (`:36`). The type holds no mutable state.
  - **`IsEmulatorConnectionString` (`:44-46`)** is the whole detection: a null-guard and a `Contains` with `StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase`, so casing in the connection string cannot smuggle a stack onto the wrong branch.
  - **`BuildAdminConnectionString` (`:59-84`)** derives the management-plane string from the AMQP one by swapping in the admin endpoint's host and port. The validation is stricter than "is it an absolute URI" and says why in a comment (`:61-63`): `localhost:5300` *is* an absolute URI, with `localhost` read as the scheme and no host at all, so the check also requires the scheme to be `http` or `https` (`:64-67`). A missing or malformed endpoint throws an `InvalidOperationException` whose message names the setting, the reason (the transport provisions topics, subscriptions and queues through a management-plane client, which the emulator serves on a different port) and the fix, including that an Aspire AppHost gets it for free (`:69-70`). The rebuild itself formats `Endpoint=sb://{host}:{port}` with the invariant culture (`:73-75`), then splits on `;` and replaces only the `Endpoint=` segment, leaving everything else in place (`:77-83`). That is what keeps the shared-access key name and value byte-identical across both clients: composing a fresh second string is one silent typo away from an admin client that cannot provision anything (`:48-53`).
  - **Where the quota is applied.** This type only defines `EmulatorEntityQuota`; [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner) sets it as `DefaultMessageTimeToLive` on each topic, queue and subscription it creates when the endpoint is the emulator (`AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner.cs:92-100`, `:111-124`, `:135-144`). Nothing process-global is changed, so a production entity keeps the service defaults.

- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-066](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/066-broker-transport-selection.html) records the local Service Bus emulator path as part of transport selection and dev/prod parity. Keeping detection and the admin-string derivation in one internal static type is what lets [AzureServiceBusTransport](#azureservicebustransport)`.ResolveEndpoint` read the emulator case as one explicit branch that produces an ordinary [AzureServiceBusEndpoint](#azureservicebusendpoint), after which nothing else in the transport needs to know.

- **Where it's used**: [AzureServiceBusTransport](#azureservicebustransport)`.ResolveEndpoint` calls `IsEmulatorConnectionString` and, on a match, `BuildAdminConnectionString(connectionString, context.Settings.EmulatorAdminEndpoint)` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:145-153`); [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner) reads `EmulatorEntityQuota`. The admin endpoint is bound from `MessageBus:EmulatorAdminEndpoint` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Messaging/MessageBusSettings.cs:54`), which an Aspire AppHost sets from the emulator resource (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire.Hosting/Extensions.cs:352`). The test tier's emulator fixture, [ServiceBusEmulatorFixtureBase](group-29-testing-infrastructure.md#per-project-test-rollup), pins the emulator image (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Azure/Fixtures/ServiceBusEmulatorFixtureBase.cs:64`). `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/ServiceBusEmulatorSupportTests.cs` covers marker detection, the endpoint swap, the loud failure on a missing or non-HTTP admin endpoint, and the quota constant.

- **Caveats / not-in-source**: whether a given AppHost run uses the emulator or RabbitMQ is a host and environment decision, so "which transport a developer is on right now" is Not determinable from source here; the source only settles that the emulator branch is reachable exactly when the resolved connection string carries the marker and no fully qualified namespace is configured.

---

### AzureServiceBusConnection
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusConnection.cs:11` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed, `IAsyncDisposable`)

- **What it is**: the process-wide Service Bus clients: one AMQP connection shared by the publisher and the receiver, plus the management-plane client. It is a singleton the container disposes on shutdown, which closes every sender and processor created from it (`AzureServiceBusConnection.cs:6-10`).
- **Depends on**: [AzureServiceBusEndpoint](#azureservicebusendpoint); externals `ServiceBusClient`, `ServiceBusAdministrationClient`.
- **Concept introduced**: `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses connection reuse: a `ServiceBusClient` owns one AMQP connection, so sharing a single instance across publishing and receiving keeps a host at one connection regardless of how many topics it sends to.
- **Walkthrough**: the constructor (`:15-22`) null-guards the endpoint, stores it as `Endpoint` (`:25`), and creates `Client` (`:28`) and `Administration` (`:31`) through the endpoint's factory methods. `DisposeAsync` (`:34`) disposes only the data-plane client; the administration client is HTTP-based and holds no connection to close.
- **Where it's used**: registered as a singleton by `AzureServiceBusTransport.Register` (`AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:81`); its `Administration` feeds [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner) (`:85-88`), its `Client` feeds [AzureServiceBusPublisher](#azureservicebuspublisher) (`:91-93`), and the whole instance is handed to [AzureServiceBusReceiver](#azureservicebusreceiver) (`:95-96`).

### ProcessorSettlement
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusReceiver.cs:202` · Level 1 · class (private sealed, nested in [AzureServiceBusReceiver](#azureservicebusreceiver))

- **What it is**: the receiver's [IAzureServiceBusSettlement](#iazureservicebussettlement): a primary-constructor class over the processor's `ProcessMessageEventArgs` and the host queue's `ServiceBusSender` (`AzureServiceBusReceiver.cs:202`).
- **Depends on**: [IAzureServiceBusSettlement](#iazureservicebussettlement); externals `ProcessMessageEventArgs`, `ServiceBusSender`.
- **Concept introduced**: none new; it is the production adapter behind the testability split that [IAzureServiceBusSettlement](#iazureservicebussettlement) introduces.
- **Walkthrough**: each member is one SDK call. `CompleteAsync` calls `args.CompleteMessageAsync` (`:204-205`), `DeadLetterAsync` calls `args.DeadLetterMessageAsync` with the reason and description (`:207-208`), `AbandonAsync` calls `args.AbandonMessageAsync` with no property changes (`:210-211`), and `ScheduleOnQueueAsync` calls `queueSender.ScheduleMessageAsync` (`:213-214`), so a redelivery lands on the same queue the processor reads.
- **Where it's used**: created per message in `AzureServiceBusReceiver.ExecuteAsync` (`AzureServiceBusReceiver.cs:115-116`) and passed to [AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler](#azureservicebusdeliveryhandler)`.HandleAsync`. No usage outside its defining file.

### AzureServiceBusMessageMapping
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusMessageMapping.cs:12` · Level 2 · class (internal, static)

- **What it is**: the translation between the framework's broker messages and Service Bus messages. Every framework header travels as a string application property under its own key, so the receiver hands the pipeline exactly the keys and values the publisher wrote (`AzureServiceBusMessageMapping.cs:7-11`).
- **Depends on**: [OutgoingBrokerMessage](group-14-module-system-composition.md#outgoingbrokermessage), [IncomingBrokerMessage](group-14-module-system-composition.md#incomingbrokermessage), [BrokerHeaders](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokerheaders); externals `ServiceBusMessage`, `ServiceBusReceivedMessage`, `CultureInfo`.
- **Concept introduced**: `[Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration]` assesses whether the wire contract is explicit and transport-independent: the framework owns the headers, and this mapping only moves them into the one place Service Bus carries arbitrary metadata, so the same message reads identically on every transport.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`ToServiceBusMessage` (`:17-34`)** builds the message from the body, sets `MessageId` to the framework message id formatted `"D"` with the invariant culture, `ContentType` to `BrokerHeaders.JsonContentType` and `Subject` to the event name (`:21-26`), then copies every header into `ApplicationProperties` (`:28-31`).
  - **`ToHeaders` (`:43-58`)** reads application properties back as an ordinal dictionary. A null value is skipped before conversion because `Convert.ToString` would turn it into an empty string; a non-string value written by another publisher is formatted with the invariant culture (`:51-53`).
  - **`ToIncoming` (`:63-71`)** produces the [IncomingBrokerMessage](group-14-module-system-composition.md#incomingbrokermessage) the pipeline processes from the body, the headers and the message id (empty when absent) (`:67-70`).
  - **`CreateRedelivery` (`:81-100`)** copies body, message id, content type and subject (`:85-90`) and every application property (`:92-95`), then overwrites `BrokerHeaders.RedeliveryCount` with the new count (`:97`). Keeping the original message id is deliberate: the consumer inbox de-duplicates on it.
- **Where it's used**: [AzureServiceBusPublisher](#azureservicebuspublisher) (`AzureServiceBusPublisher.cs:44`) and [AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler](#azureservicebusdeliveryhandler) (`AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler.cs:61`, `:76`). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusMessageMappingTests.cs`.

---

### AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler.cs:24` · Level 3 · class (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the step between a delivered Service Bus message and the framework's [IBrokerReceivePipeline](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerreceivepipeline): it hands the message in and carries out the [BrokerDisposition](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokerdisposition) that comes back. It decides nothing itself (`AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler.cs:7-23`). The Amazon counterpart is [AmazonSqsDeliveryHandler](#amazonsqsdeliveryhandler).
- **Depends on**: [IBrokerReceivePipeline](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerreceivepipeline), [BrokerDisposition](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokerdisposition), [IAzureServiceBusSettlement](#iazureservicebussettlement), [AzureServiceBusMessageMapping](#azureservicebusmessagemapping); externals `TimeProvider`, `ILogger`.
- **Concept introduced, settle by disposition, never by judgment.** `[Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration]` assesses whether retry and poison handling are consistent across transports: the policy (in-process retry, second-level redelivery, dead-lettering) lives once in the shared pipeline, and each transport only maps its three outcomes onto native calls. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` applies to the order of those calls: a redelivery schedules the copy *before* completing the original, so a crash in between yields a duplicate the inbox absorbs, never a lost message (`:13-15`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **State.** `MaxDeadLetterTextLength = 4096` (`:27`), the longest reason or description the SDK accepts; the pipeline, a `TimeProvider` and a logger (`:29-31`), all null-guarded in the constructor (`:37`).
  - **`HandleAsync` (`:53-95`)** maps the message and calls `_pipeline.ProcessAsync` (`:61`). An `OperationCanceledException` raised because the host is stopping is caught and turned into a best-effort abandon (`:63-67`). Otherwise it switches on the disposition: `Completed` completes (`:71-73`); `Redeliver` schedules `CreateRedelivery(message, redeliver.RedeliveryCount)` at `_timeProvider.GetUtcNow() + redeliver.Delay` and then completes the original (`:75-81`); `DeadLetter` dead-letters with the truncated reason and detail (`:83-88`); any other disposition throws `InvalidOperationException`, treated as a framework bug so the processor abandons and reports rather than guesses (`:90-93`). Every settle call passes `CancellationToken.None`: the work is done, and settling it during shutdown is what keeps it from running twice (`:21-22`).
  - **`Truncate` (`:100-101`)** cuts text longer than 4096 characters.
  - **`AbandonBestEffortAsync` (`:103-115`)** swallows and logs any abandon failure under a scoped `CA1031` suppression (`:109`): an unabandoned message is redelivered once its lock expires anyway. `LogAbandonFailed` is event id 10 at Warning (`:117-118`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-087](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/087-broker-poison-message-handling.html) records second-level redelivery and poison handling, applied unconditionally on Service Bus because it schedules natively.
- **Where it's used**: constructed per host by `AzureServiceBusTransport.Register` (`AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:98-101`) and invoked by [AzureServiceBusReceiver](#azureservicebusreceiver) for every message (`AzureServiceBusReceiver.cs:115-116`). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandlerTests.cs`.

---

### AzureServiceBusEntityNames
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusEntityNames.cs:11` · Level 3 · class (internal, static)

- **What it is**: the Service Bus entity names the transport derives from the framework's broker names. Service Bus caps a queue or topic path at 260 characters and a subscription name at 50, so each name goes through [BrokerNaming](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokernaming)`.Fit`: unchanged when it fits, otherwise shortened deterministically so the publisher and every consumer compute the same entity (`AzureServiceBusEntityNames.cs:5-10`). Sibling of [AmazonSqsEntityNames](#amazonsqsentitynames).
- **Depends on**: [BrokerNaming](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokernaming).
- **Concept introduced**: none new; deterministic fitting is taught at [BrokerNaming](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokernaming). `[Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration]` applies: independent hosts agree on entity names without coordination.
- **Walkthrough**: `MaxEntityPathLength = 260` (`:14`) and `MaxSubscriptionNameLength = 50` (`:17`). `Queue(queueName)` (`:22`) and `Topic(topic)` (`:27`) fit to 260; `Subscription(queueName)` (`:35`) fits the *queue* name to 50, so each consuming host owns exactly one subscription per topic, named after its queue.
- **Where it's used**: [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner), [AzureServiceBusPublisher](#azureservicebuspublisher) and [AzureServiceBusReceiver](#azureservicebusreceiver). The same names are asserted against the deployed infrastructure template by `MMCA.ADC/Tests/Services/MMCA.ADC.Services.Tests/Messaging/BrokerTopologyBicepTests.cs`, and in Common by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTopologyTests.cs`.

---

### AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner.cs:17` · Level 4 · class (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: creates the entities a host's [BrokerTopology](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertopology) needs, idempotently, through the management plane: the host's queue, a topic per consumed event, and on each topic one subscription named after the queue that forwards every message to it. Existing entities are left exactly as they are, and a topic once seen is cached, so a steady-state publish costs no management call (`AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner.cs:9-16`). Sibling of [AmazonSqsTopologyProvisioner](#amazonsqstopologyprovisioner).
- **Depends on**: [BrokerTopology](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertopology), [AzureServiceBusEntityNames](#azureservicebusentitynames), [ServiceBusEmulatorSupport](#servicebusemulatorsupport); externals `ServiceBusAdministrationClient`, `ServiceBusException`, `ConcurrentDictionary`.
- **Concept introduced, topic fan-out with auto-forwarding subscriptions.** `[Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration]` assesses the publish/subscribe topology: a publisher only knows a topic per event; each consuming host adds one subscription that auto-forwards into its own queue, so a new consumer never changes a publisher and one queue receives every event the host consumes. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` applies to concurrent startup: an exists-check followed by a create that absorbs `MessagingEntityAlreadyExists` makes two hosts (or two first publishes) racing for one entity both succeed.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **State.** The admin client, the `isEmulator` flag, a logger, and `_knownTopics`, an ordinal `ConcurrentDictionary` used as a set (`:19-22`); constructor at `:28`.
  - **`ProvisionAsync` (`:45-65`)** returns immediately for a topology with no subscriptions, since a host that only publishes owns no queue (`:49-52`). It computes the queue and subscription names (`:54-55`), creates the queue first because a subscription cannot forward to an entity that does not exist yet (`:58`), then for each distinct topic ensures the topic and its subscription (`:60-64`).
  - **`EnsureTopicAsync` (`:71-86`)** returns on a cache hit (`:73`), otherwise checks existence, creates when missing and logs only when this call created it (`:78-83`), then caches the topic (`:85`).
  - **The option builders** are `internal static` so tests can assert them. `BuildTopicOptions` (`:92`) and `BuildSubscriptionOptions` (`:135`, `ForwardTo` at `:137`) only add the emulator's one-hour `DefaultMessageTimeToLive`; `BuildQueueOptions` (`:111`) also sets `DeadLetteringOnMessageExpiration = true` and `RequiresDuplicateDetection = false` (`:115-116`), the latter because a scheduled redelivery deliberately reuses the original message id.
  - **`EnsureQueueAsync` (`:146`)** and **`EnsureSubscriptionAsync` (`:156`)** follow the same check-then-create shape. **`CreateUnlessExistsAsync` (`:171-182`)** returns `true` when it created the entity and `false` on the `MessagingEntityAlreadyExists` conflict (`:178`). Logs are event ids 1 and 2 at Information (`:184-188`).
- **Why it's built this way**: provisioning is on by default (`MessageBus:ProvisionTopology`, `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Infrastructure/Messaging/MessageBusSettings.cs:84`) so a development stack or emulator needs no setup; turning it off lets a deployment own the entities in infrastructure code instead, which the ADC bicep test pins against the same names.
- **Where it's used**: registered only when `settings.ProvisionTopology` is true (`AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:83-89`); called by [AzureServiceBusReceiver](#azureservicebusreceiver) at startup and by [AzureServiceBusPublisher](#azureservicebuspublisher) before a first send. Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTopologyTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: whether each deployed environment runs with provisioning on or off is configuration, Not determinable from source.

---

### AzureServiceBusPublisher
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusPublisher.cs:13` · Level 5 · class (internal sealed, `IBrokerPublisher`, `IAsyncDisposable`)

- **What it is**: the Azure Service Bus [IBrokerPublisher](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerpublisher): sends each message to its event's topic through one cached `ServiceBusSender` per topic. A send the broker rejects throws, so the outbox retries the row (`AzureServiceBusPublisher.cs:7-12`). Sibling of [AmazonSqsPublisher](#amazonsqspublisher).
- **Depends on**: [IBrokerPublisher](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerpublisher), [OutgoingBrokerMessage](group-14-module-system-composition.md#outgoingbrokermessage), [AzureServiceBusEntityNames](#azureservicebusentitynames), [AzureServiceBusMessageMapping](#azureservicebusmessagemapping), [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner) (optional); externals `ServiceBusClient`, `ServiceBusSender`.
- **Concept introduced**: none new; the outbox's at-least-once publish is taught in the primer (ADR-003). `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` applies: senders are cached per topic so a steady-state publish opens no new link.
- **Walkthrough**: fields are the shared client, the optional provisioner and an ordinal `ConcurrentDictionary<string, ServiceBusSender>` (`:15-17`); the constructor null-guards only the client (`:22`). `PublishAsync` (`:31-45`) fits the topic name (`:35`), ensures the topic exists when a provisioner is present (`:37-40`), gets or creates the sender with a static lambda that captures nothing (`:42`), and sends the mapped message (`:44`). `DisposeAsync` (`:48-56`) disposes every cached sender and clears the cache.
- **Where it's used**: registered as the host's `IBrokerPublisher` singleton (`AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:91-93`), which the outbox processor uses. Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransportTests.cs`.

### AzureServiceBusReceiver
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusReceiver.cs:19` · Level 5 · class (internal sealed partial, `BackgroundService`)

- **What it is**: the hosted receiver of the Azure Service Bus transport. In the background, so a broker still warming up or briefly unreachable never fails host startup, it provisions the topology when provisioning is on (retrying with backoff until it succeeds), then runs a `ServiceBusProcessor` on the host's queue that hands every message to [AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler](#azureservicebusdeliveryhandler). A host that consumes no event creates no queue and runs no processor (`AzureServiceBusReceiver.cs:10-18`). Sibling of [AmazonSqsReceiver](#amazonsqsreceiver).
- **Depends on**: [AzureServiceBusConnection](#azureservicebusconnection), [BrokerTopology](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertopology), [AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler](#azureservicebusdeliveryhandler), [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner) (optional), [MessageBusSettings](group-14-module-system-composition.md#messagebussettings), [BrokerTransportPolicies](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertransportpolicies), [AzureServiceBusEntityNames](#azureservicebusentitynames); nested [ProcessorSettlement](#processorsettlement). Externals: `BackgroundService`, `ServiceBusProcessor`.
- **Concept introduced, lock duration sized to the retry budget.** `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses backpressure: concurrency and prefetch come from shared settings, and prefetch defaults to zero on purpose, because a prefetched message's lock runs while it waits and the pipeline can hold a handler for tens of seconds of in-process retry (`:24-29`). `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` applies to lock renewal: 15 minutes is far beyond the default in-process retry budget (1 + 2 + 4 + 8 + 16 seconds plus handler time), so a message being retried is never handed to a second consumer (`:31-36`). `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]` applies to the log contract: a shutdown cancellation is not reported as an error.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Constants.** `DefaultMaxConcurrentCalls = 16` (`:22`), `DefaultPrefetchCount = 0` (`:29`), `MaxAutoLockRenewalDuration` = 15 minutes (`:36`), provisioning backoff from 2 seconds to 1 minute (`:38-39`). The constructor null-guards everything but the optional provisioner (`:55-75`).
  - **`BuildProcessorOptions` (`:80-92`)** sets `PeekLock` and `AutoCompleteMessages = false` (`:86-87`) so the handler settles explicitly, resolves prefetch and concurrency through `BrokerTransportPolicies` with the defaults above as fallback (`:88-89`; settings `MessageBusSettings.cs:223`, `:232`), and applies the lock renewal (`:90`).
  - **`ExecuteAsync` (`:95-138`)** returns for an empty topology (`:97-100`) and when provisioning is configured but the host stopped first (`:104-107`). It creates a sender on its own queue for redeliveries (`:109`) and the processor (`:112`), wires each message to `_handler.HandleAsync` with a fresh [ProcessorSettlement](#processorsettlement) (`:115-116`) and errors to `OnProcessErrorAsync` (`:117`), starts processing, logs, and waits on an infinite delay until stopping (`:121-123`). The `finally` stops the processor if it is still running (`:129-134`), and the `await using` blocks dispose processor and sender.
  - **`ProvisionWithRetryAsync` (`:145-179`)** loops: provision, return `true` on success, return `false` on a stop-driven cancellation, otherwise log (under a scoped `CA1031` suppression at `:161`) and delay, doubling the backoff up to the one-minute cap (`:177`).
  - **`OnProcessErrorAsync` (`:181-190`)** logs unless the exception is a cancellation raised while stopping (`:184`). Log event ids are 20 (Information), 21 and 22 (Error) (`:192-199`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-087](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/087-broker-poison-message-handling.html) records the receive and poison policies the handler carries out; this class keeps only the Service Bus processor mechanics.
- **Where it's used**: registered as a hosted service by `AzureServiceBusTransport.Register` (`AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:95-104`). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusReceiverTests.cs`.

### AzureServiceBusTransport
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Messaging` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:37` · Level 6 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the [IBrokerTransport](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokertransport) for Azure Service Bus, selected when `MessageBus:Provider` is `AzureServiceBus`, built on the `Azure.Messaging.ServiceBus` SDK alone. It resolves where to connect and registers the connection, the optional provisioner, the publisher and the hosted receiver (`AzureServiceBusTransport.cs:37-214`).
- **Depends on**: [IBrokerTransport](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokertransport), [BrokerTransportContext](group-14-module-system-composition.md#brokertransportcontext) (settings, configuration, topology and resolved connection string), [IBrokerReceivePipeline](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerreceivepipeline), [IBrokerPublisher](group-14-module-system-composition.md#ibrokerpublisher), [AzureTokenCredentials](#azuretokencredentials), [AzureServiceBusTransportSettings](#azureservicebustransportsettings), [AzureServiceBusEndpoint](#azureservicebusendpoint), [AzureServiceBusConnection](#azureservicebusconnection), [AzureServiceBusTopologyProvisioner](#azureservicebustopologyprovisioner), [AzureServiceBusPublisher](#azureservicebuspublisher), [AzureServiceBusReceiver](#azureservicebusreceiver), [AzureServiceBusDeliveryHandler](#azureservicebusdeliveryhandler), [ServiceBusEmulatorSupport](#servicebusemulatorsupport). Externals: `Azure.Core.TokenCredential`, `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection`, `Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration`.
- **Concept introduced, a pluggable broker transport per vendor.** `[Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration]` assesses whether the broker is a replaceable detail behind the application's messaging: the core picks a transport by name, and this class is the Azure one, living in the vendor assembly beside [AmazonSqsTransport](#amazonsqstransport). `[Rubric §11, Security]` applies to sign-in: a configured namespace uses a Microsoft Entra token, so the namespace can run with shared access keys switched off, and that path wins over any connection string. `[Rubric §17, DevOps]` applies through the emulator branch: a development stack runs the same transport as production.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Identity.** `ProviderName = "AzureServiceBus"` (`:40`) is returned by `Name` (`:59`). `FullyQualifiedNamespaceKey` composes the configuration key for error messages (`:43`). `MaxRedeliveryDelay` is `null` (`:62`): Service Bus schedules arbitrarily far ahead, so the shared pipeline applies no cap.
  - **Credential factory.** The public constructor delegates to `AzureTokenCredentials.Create` (`:48-51`); an internal constructor takes any `Func<string?, TokenCredential>` for tests (`:55-56`), reached through `CreateCredential` (`:166`).
  - **`Register` (`:69-105`)** resolves the endpoint *during* `AddBrokerMessaging`, so a configuration error fails startup rather than the first publish (`:74-76`). It then `TryAdd`s `TimeProvider.System` (`:80`), registers [AzureServiceBusConnection](#azureservicebusconnection) as a singleton (`:81`), the provisioner only when `settings.ProvisionTopology` is on (`:83-89`), the publisher as `IBrokerPublisher` (`:91-93`), and the receiver as a hosted service with a new delivery handler over the host's `IBrokerReceivePipeline` (`:95-104`).
  - **`ResolveEndpoint` (`:115-161`)** reads [AzureServiceBusTransportSettings](#azureservicebustransportsettings) (`:119`). A configured namespace returns a token endpoint with no connection strings (`:122-131`). With neither namespace nor connection string it throws an `InvalidOperationException` naming both options and the connection-string keys (`:133-140`). An emulator connection string returns an endpoint with the derived admin string and `isEmulator: true` (`:145-153`). Otherwise the same connection string serves both planes (`:155-160`).
  - **`ReadSettings` (`:171-177`)** binds `MessageBus:AzureServiceBus`, falling back to defaults when the section is absent.
  - **`ResolveNamespaceAddress` (`:186-204`)** returns `null` for blank input, takes the host from an `sb://` address (via `HostOfServiceBusAddress`, `:206-210`) or trims a trailing slash from a bare host (`:194`), rejects anything `Uri.CheckHostName` does not classify as DNS, IPv4 or IPv6 with an error naming the key (`:196-201`), and returns `sb://{host}/` (`:203`). `LoggerFor<T>` falls back to `NullLoggerFactory` when no logger factory is registered (`:212-213`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-066](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/066-broker-transport-selection.html) records transport selection by provider name and dev/prod parity; [ADR-087](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/087-broker-poison-message-handling.html) the redelivery and poison policies, applied unconditionally on Service Bus; [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html) the cloud-neutral core with vendor adapter packages, including managed-identity sign-in for this transport; [ADR-118](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/118-message-transport-library-policy.html) the message-transport library policy. Keeping the vendor transport out of the core keeps the Azure SDK out of hosts that do not use it, which the architecture rules check by listing the `Azure` namespace root among cloud-vendor dependencies (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Testing.Architecture/Rules/Layering/ArchitectureRules.CloudVendors.cs:32`).
- **Where it's used**: registered by [AzureServiceCollectionExtensions](#azureservicecollectionextensions)`.AddAzureServiceBusTransport` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:154-155`), documented to be called before `AddBrokerMessaging` (`:139-152`). Every MMCA.ADC service host registers it through `RegisterBrokerTransports` (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:260`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:325`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Service/Program.cs:140`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Notification.Service/Program.cs:97`). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Messaging/AzureServiceBusTransportTests.cs` and `AzureServiceBusTransportCredentialTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: which provider and which sign-in path each deployment selects is configuration, Not determinable from source.

### AzureNotificationHubSettings
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Notifications/AzureNotificationHubSettings.cs:9` · Level 0 · class (public sealed)

- **What it is**: the options class bound from the `NativePush` section that switches Azure Notification Hubs delivery on: an `Enabled` flag, a connection string and a hub name (`AzureNotificationHubSettings.cs:9-22`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party.
- **Concept**: configuration as the on switch. `[Rubric §17, DevOps]` assesses whether a capability can be enabled per environment without a code change: the host registers the channel unconditionally and the section decides (see [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html), [ADR-070](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/070-fail-fast-configuration-contract.html)).
- **Walkthrough**: `SectionName` is `"NativePush"` (`:12`); `Enabled` (`:15`), `ConnectionString` (`:18`, documented as a Listen+Send+Manage rule) and `HubName` (`:21`) are `init`-only.
- **Why it's built this way**: the registration doc says the hub is provisioned before its FCM/APNs credentials exist, so a deployment needs to hold the section and keep the channel off until the platform credentials are in place (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:76-92`).
- **Where it's used**: `AddAzureNotificationHubsPush` binds it (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:99-102`) and returns without registering anything unless `Enabled` is true and both strings are non-blank (`:91-96`).

### NativePushUserTags
> MMCA.Common.Azure · `MMCA.Common.Azure.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Notifications/NativePushUserTags.cs:8` · Level 0 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: the owner-tag vocabulary of the Azure Notification Hubs adapter: how a user id becomes a `user:{id}` installation tag, and how a set of users becomes the OR tag expressions a send targets (`NativePushUserTags.cs:8-29`).
- **Depends on**: the `UserIdentifierType` alias; BCL `string.Create` with `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`.
- **Concept introduced, addressing users, not devices.** `[Rubric §10, Messaging & Integration]` assesses whether an integration hides vendor addressing behind application concepts: callers send to user ids, and this type translates them into the hub's tag language. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` applies through the chunking: Azure Notification Hubs caps a tag expression at 20 tags, so a large audience becomes several expressions rather than one rejected send (`:10-11`).
- **Walkthrough**: `MaxTagsPerExpression = 20` (`:11`). `BuildUserTagExpressions` maps every id through `UserTag`, chunks by 20, and joins each chunk with `" || "` (`:17-21`). `UserTagPrefix` is `"user:"` (`:24`), and `UserTag` formats the id invariantly behind that prefix (`:27-28`), so the tag stamped at registration and the tag targeted at send time are byte-identical.
- **Why it's built this way**: one owner of the tag format keeps the registrar and the sender from drifting apart; a different format on either side would silently deliver to nobody. [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html) records the hub as the device registry this tagging relies on.
- **Where it's used**: [AzureNotificationHubNativePushSender](#azurenotificationhubnativepushsender) loops `BuildUserTagExpressions` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Notifications/AzureNotificationHubNativePushSender.cs:43`); [AzureNotificationHubDeviceRegistrar](#azurenotificationhubdeviceregistrar) stamps `UserTag` on each installation and uses `UserTagPrefix` to recognise another owner (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/Notifications/AzureNotificationHubDeviceRegistrar.cs:41`, `:125-128`). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Notifications/NativePushUserTagsTests.cs`.
