  - `IsConfigured` is a constant `true` (`AzureBlobFileStorageService.cs:25`). Its purpose is to differ from the `false` on [NullFileStorageService](group-14-module-system-composition.md#nullfilestorageservice).
  - `UploadAsync(blobName, content, contentType, ct)` (`:29-30`) forwards to the options overload with `FileUploadOptions.None`. Both overloads carry an `RS0026` suppression (`:28`, `:33`): the pair is grandfathered on the interface, and the justification records that the type moved here from `MMCA.Common.Infrastructure`, so both overloads entered this package's public API as a pair.
  - The real work is `UploadAsync(blobName, content, contentType, options, ct)` (`:34-72`). It null-guards `options` (`:36`), resolves a blob client (`:40`), and uploads with `BlobUploadOptions` carrying the content type plus `options.ContentDisposition` and `options.CacheControl` as HTTP headers (`:41-52`), returning the blob's URI on success (`:54`).
  - Two catches yield the same `Error.Failure` with code `FileStorage.UploadFailed`: `RequestFailedException`, the Azure SDK's service failure (`:56-63`), and any exception `IsTransportFailure` accepts (`:64-71`).
  - `IsTransportFailure` (`:107-109`) accepts `HttpRequestException`, `IOException`, or an `OperationCanceledException` the caller did not request (a timeout after the client's own retries, `:101-105`). The caller's own cancellation is excluded and keeps propagating.
  - `DeleteAsync(blobName, ct)` (`:75-99`) uses `DeleteBlobIfExistsAsync` (`:79`), so an unknown blob name is success, which is what the interface promises ("unknown names succeed (idempotent)", `MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/Interfaces/Infrastructure/Storage/IFileStorageService.cs:43`). The same two catches yield `FileStorage.DeleteFailed` (`:83-98`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html) records blob storage as the home for binary content the databases should not hold, and [ADR-123](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/123-speaker-session-assets.html) extends it to speaker and session assets. The registration is where the credential story lives (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:44-74`): an absolute `ServiceUri` with `DefaultAzureCredential` is the managed-identity production path, `ConnectionString` is the local Azurite path, and `ContainerName` is required for either (`:49-66`). An incomplete section returns before registering anything, which leaves the null default in place, so a host can call `AddAzureBlobFileStorage` unconditionally.
- **Where it's used**: registered by `AddAzureBlobFileStorage` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure/AzureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:71`), which MMCA.ADC's Identity and Conference service hosts call (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Service/Program.cs:261`, `MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:367`). Consumers depend on [IFileStorageService](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ifilestorageservice) only. The S3 and GCS siblings are [S3FileStorageService](#s3filestorageservice) and [GcsFileStorageService](#gcsfilestorageservice). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Azure.Tests/Storage/AzureBlobFileStorageServiceTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: whether the configured container is public-read or served through a signed URL is a provisioning decision, Not determinable from source.

---

### FcmInstallation
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmInstallation.cs:13` · Level 0 · record (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the in-memory shape of one Firestore installation document: `InstallationId`, `UserId`, `Platform`, `PushChannel` (`FcmInstallation.cs:13-17`), where `PushChannel` is the FCM registration token.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party beyond the `UserIdentifierType` alias (see the identifier-type aliases in the [primer](00-primer.md#4-c-build-and-code-style-conventions)); `System.Globalization`.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`FirestoreInstallationRegistry`](#firestoreinstallationregistry), which explains why FCM needs a side registry at all (an FCM token carries no owner). It is the GCP twin of [`SnsInstallation`](#snsinstallation), minus the SNS endpoint and subscription ARNs: FCM addresses the token directly and topic membership lives in FCM, not in the row.
- **Walkthrough**: a positional record with no nullable members (`FcmInstallation.cs:13-17`). `UserKey(userId)` (`FcmInstallation.cs:22-23`) renders a user id as an invariant-culture string with `string.Create(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, ...)`; that string is what the `UserId` field stores and what queries filter on, so every writer and reader goes through this one method.
- **Where it's used**: materialized by `FirestoreInstallationRegistry.FromSnapshot` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:109-113`), built by [`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar`](#fcmpushdeviceregistrar) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:112`), grouped by token in [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSender.cs:43-45`), and the element type of every [`IFcmInstallationRegistry`](#ifcminstallationregistry) member.

### FcmNativePushSettings
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSettings.cs:10` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the bound options for Firebase Cloud Messaging push, section `NativePush:Fcm` (`FcmNativePushSettings.cs:13`), gated by the shared sibling key `NativePush:Enabled` (`FcmNativePushSettings.cs:16`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`GcpServiceCollectionExtensions`](#gcpservicecollectionextensions) (`AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPush`) for the gate-plus-completeness registration pattern this class feeds; the AWS sibling is [`SnsNativePushSettings`](#snsnativepushsettings). `[Rubric §15, Best Practices & Code Quality]` assesses whether configuration is explicit and safe by default; here the pipeline ships inert and is switched on by configuration alone, because the Firebase project and the Firestore database are provisioned outside the application (`FcmNativePushSettings.cs:6-8`).
- **Walkthrough**: `ProjectId` (`FcmNativePushSettings.cs:22`) is the Google Cloud project that owns both the Firebase app (the FCM HTTP v1 sender) and the Firestore database holding the installation registry (`FcmNativePushSettings.cs:18-21`); `InstallationsCollection` (`FcmNativePushSettings.cs:25`) is the Firestore collection, one document per installation. `IsComplete` (`FcmNativePushSettings.cs:28-30`) requires both to be non-blank. There is no topic setting: delivery is per registration token only.
- **Why it's built this way**: unlike SNS there is no credential or endpoint setting; the registration doc comment states credentials come from Application Default Credentials only, so there is no key-file setting (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:133-135`). A gate that is off, or a section missing either value, makes the registration a no-op, so hosts call it unconditionally (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:130-133`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: `AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPush` reads the gate and section, returns early unless the gate parses true and `IsComplete` holds, then binds the options (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:153-162`); [`FcmPushClientFactory`](#fcmpushclientfactory) takes it directly; [`FirestoreInstallationRegistry`](#firestoreinstallationregistry) reads it through `IOptions<FcmNativePushSettings>`. Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Notifications/AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPushTests.cs`.

### FcmSendOutcome
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmSendOutcome.cs:12` · Level 0 · record (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the result of one message in an FCM batch send: `IsSuccess`, an optional FirebaseAdmin `MessagingErrorCode`, and the optional exception (`FcmSendOutcome.cs:12`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; FirebaseAdmin `MessagingErrorCode`.
- **Concept**: a vendor-neutral-to-this-package outcome type, so the sender can be tested against a fake [`IFcmMessagingClient`](#ifcmmessagingclient) without constructing FirebaseAdmin's own batch response. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether a failure is classified before it is acted on; here only a failure that indicts the token prunes data.
- **Walkthrough**: `Success` (`FcmSendOutcome.cs:15`) is the shared success instance. `IsStaleToken` (`FcmSendOutcome.cs:25-28`) is true for `MessagingErrorCode.Unregistered`, or for `InvalidArgument` only when the exception message contains "registration token" (ordinal, case-insensitive).
- **Why it's built this way**: the doc comment records the asymmetry (`FcmSendOutcome.cs:17-24`): FCM answers a malformed token with `InvalidArgument` and a message naming the registration token, but any other `InvalidArgument` is about the message itself, would fail every token alike, and must never prune them. See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: produced by [`FirebaseMessagingClient`](#firebasemessagingclient) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:31-33`) and by the test doubles `FakeFcmMessagingClient` and `RecordingFcmMessagingClient`; consumed by [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSender.cs:66-79`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the stale-token classification depends on FCM's English error text; whether that wording is stable is not determinable from source.

### FirstFailure
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSender.cs:145` · Level 0 · class (private sealed, nested in [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender))

- **What it is**: a tiny accumulator that keeps the first exception offered during one user-targeted send, which becomes the `FirstFailure` of the returned [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome) (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:144`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party.
- **Concept**: first-failure capture across a best-effort fan-out. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether partial failure is reported rather than swallowed; here the send keeps going past a failed lookup, chunk or token, yet the caller still learns that something failed and sees one concrete exception.
- **Walkthrough**: `Value` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:147`) has a private setter; `Offer(failure)` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:149`) assigns with `??=`, so the first non-null exception sticks and later offers (including `null`) are ignored.
- **Why it's built this way**: the failure points are spread over two methods (the lookup in `LookupAsync` and the send loop in `SendToUsersAsync`), so one instance is created per send and passed into `LookupAsync` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:40,91`). A dead token counts as a failure, since its device was not reached (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:21-23`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: created once per `SendToUsersAsync` call (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:39`) and offered a failed user lookup (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:103`), a failed chunk (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:59`), a stale token (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:72`) and any other failed token (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:77`); its `Value` is returned as the outcome's `FirstFailure` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:87`).

### GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Secrets` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Secrets/GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:33` · Level 0 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the `ConfigurationProvider` that loads every Google Secret Manager secret under one id prefix into `IConfiguration`, mapping `adc-prod-ConnectionStrings--Default` to `ConnectionStrings:Default` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:12-13`), with an optional timed reload.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; Google `SecretManagerServiceClient`, `Grpc.Core.RpcException`, `Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration.ConfigurationProvider`.
- **Concept**: the GCP sibling of [`AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider`](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationprovider) and of the Key Vault provider used by [`AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions`](#azurekeyvaultconfigurationextensions): one naming convention across clouds, so a secret set moves by renaming its prefix. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses whether secrets stay out of files and images and whether access is least-privilege; here values come from the vault at startup and each failure message names the exact IAM permission and role to grant. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` applies to the reload: a failed refresh keeps the last good values.
- **Walkthrough**: constants `KeyDelimiter = "--"` and `LatestVersion = "latest"` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:35-36`); state is the client, project, prefix, optional interval, a disposal `CancellationTokenSource`, a `Lock` timer gate, the timer and a disposed flag (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:38-45`). The internal constructor null-checks client, project and prefix (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:52-62`). `KeyFor(secretId, namePrefix)` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:74-82`) returns `null` unless the id starts with the prefix ordinally and is longer than it, then strips the prefix and replaces `--` with the configuration separator. `Load()` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:85-99`) sets `Data = Read()` and, with an interval, creates a one-shot `Timer` once under the gate (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:89-98`). `ReloadOnce` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:119-147`) re-reads, swaps `Data` and calls `OnReload()` only when `HasSameData` is false (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:125-129`), records any non-cancellation exception in `LastReloadFailure` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:65,131-136`), and re-arms the timer in `finally` unless disposed (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:139-145`). `Read()` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:149-186`) lists secrets with the filter `name:"{prefix}"` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:156`), keeps only ids `KeyFor` accepts (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:164`), wraps a listing `RpcException` in an `InvalidOperationException` naming `secretmanager.secrets.list` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:170-175`), then reads each value into a case-insensitive dictionary. `ReadLatest` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:188-218`) gets the `latest` version, returns `null` on `NotFound` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:197-201`) or a non-`Enabled` state (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:203-206`), and otherwise accesses the concrete version name the state check saw, decoding the payload as UTF-8 (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:210`). `Dispose()` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:102-117`) stops the timer under the gate and cancels in-flight calls.
- **Why it's built this way**: the service's name filter is a case-insensitive substring match, so the ordinal prefix re-check keeps another environment's secrets out (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:17-19`). Accessing the concrete version rather than the `latest` alias again means a rotation landing between the two calls cannot return a value whose state was never checked (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:208-209`). Every call is synchronous because `ConfigurationProvider.Load` is synchronous and the host needs the values before the services that read them are registered; the one-shot re-armed timer means two loads never overlap (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:24-30`). A throttled call or revoked grant during a reload must not take down a host that already has its values (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider.cs:133-134`). See [ADR-061](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/061-runtime-secret-management.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: built only by [`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource`](#googlesecretmanagerconfigurationsource) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Secrets/GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource.cs:31-32`); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Secrets/GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProviderTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `LastReloadFailure` is internal and nothing in the defining file surfaces it to logs or health checks; whether any caller reads it outside tests is not shown in this brief.

### GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Secrets` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Secrets/GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource.cs:15` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the `IConfigurationSource` that carries a client factory, the project id, the name prefix and the reload interval, and builds a [`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider`](#googlesecretmanagerconfigurationprovider).
- **Depends on**: [`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationProvider`](#googlesecretmanagerconfigurationprovider); Google `SecretManagerServiceClient`, `IConfigurationSource`.
- **Concept**: the standard configuration source/provider split; cross-reference [`AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationSource`](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationsource) for the identical AWS shape.
- **Walkthrough**: primary-constructor parameters `clientFactory`, `projectId`, `namePrefix`, `reloadInterval` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource.cs:15-19`), with `ProjectId`, `NamePrefix` and `ReloadInterval` exposed as internal read-only properties (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource.cs:22,25,28`). `Build` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource.cs:31-32`) invokes the factory and constructs the provider.
- **Why it's built this way**: taking a `Func<SecretManagerServiceClient>` rather than a client defers client creation to `Build`, and the internal properties let tests assert what the extension method configured without a live client.
- **Where it's used**: added by `AddGoogleSecretManagerConfiguration` with `SecretManagerServiceClient.Create` as the factory (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensions.cs:118-119`), see [`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensions`](#googlesecretmanagerconfigurationextensions); asserted by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensionsTests.cs`.

### IFcmInstallationRegistry
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:9` · Level 1 · interface (internal)

- **What it is**: the adapter-internal contract over the installation registry: read one, claim one under the ownership rule, query by users, and two conditional deletes.
- **Depends on**: [`FcmInstallation`](#fcminstallation).
- **Concept introduced, an internal port for a vendor store.** The registry sits behind an interface even though it is internal so that [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender) and [`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar`](#fcmpushdeviceregistrar) can be unit-tested against `FakeFcmInstallationRegistry` while the Firestore implementation is covered by the emulator tier (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Emulator.Tests/FcmPushFirestoreEmulatorTests.cs`). `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether infrastructure can be substituted in tests; here the substitution point is the adapter's own boundary, not the core [`IPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar).
- **Walkthrough**: `GetAsync` (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:15`) returns `null` for an unknown id. `TryClaimAsync` (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:29`) writes atomically and returns `(Claimed, PreviousPushChannel)`; it is refused only when the id exists with another owner AND a different push channel (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:17-22`), and the previous channel is `null` for a new id (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:25-28`); [`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar`](#fcmpushdeviceregistrar) reads only `Claimed` and discards the previous channel (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:54`). `QueryByUsersAsync` (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:35`) takes distinct stored user keys. `DeleteOwnedAsync` (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:42`) deletes only while the given user still owns the id; `DeleteIfPushChannelAsync` (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:52`) deletes only while the id still holds the given token, so a stale-token cleanup never removes a registration refreshed onto a new token (`IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:44-47`).
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [`FirestoreInstallationRegistry`](#firestoreinstallationregistry), registered with `TryAddSingleton` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:165`); consumed by [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender) and [`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar`](#fcmpushdeviceregistrar).

### IFcmMessagingClient
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/IFcmMessagingClient.cs:11` · Level 1 · interface (internal)

- **What it is**: the adapter-internal wrapper over the one FirebaseAdmin messaging call this package uses: the batch send `SendEachAsync`.
- **Depends on**: [`FcmSendOutcome`](#fcmsendoutcome); FirebaseAdmin `Message`.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`IFcmInstallationRegistry`](#ifcminstallationregistry) for the internal-port rationale. The doc comment states why the port exists: FCM has no emulator and FirebaseAdmin's response and exception types cannot be constructed outside the SDK, so the sender talks to this interface and its rules are unit-tested against a fake (`IFcmMessagingClient.cs:6-8`), here `FakeFcmMessagingClient` or `RecordingFcmMessagingClient`.
- **Walkthrough**: `MaxMessagesPerSend = 500` (`IFcmMessagingClient.cs:17`), FirebaseAdmin's own limit on `SendEachAsync` (`IFcmMessagingClient.cs:13-16`). `SendEachAsync` (`IFcmMessagingClient.cs:23`) sends each message as its own FCM HTTP v1 request and returns one outcome per message, in order (`IFcmMessagingClient.cs:19-22`).
- **Why it's built this way**: returning per-message [`FcmSendOutcome`](#fcmsendoutcome) values rather than FirebaseAdmin's batch response keeps the sender's classification rules (success, stale token, other failure) testable without the SDK. See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [`FirebaseMessagingClient`](#firebasemessagingclient), registered as a `TryAddSingleton` factory (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:164`); consumed by [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSender.cs:48,54`).

### FirebaseMessagingClient
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:17` · Level 2 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the production [`IFcmMessagingClient`](#ifcmmessagingclient), which lazily creates its own uniquely named `FirebaseApp` over Application Default Credentials and delegates to `FirebaseMessaging`.
- **Depends on**: [`IFcmMessagingClient`](#ifcmmessagingclient), [`FcmSendOutcome`](#fcmsendoutcome); FirebaseAdmin `FirebaseApp`, `FirebaseMessaging`; Google `GoogleCredential`.
- **Concept**: lazy, serialized initialization of a process-global resource. `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]` assesses whether patterns solve a real problem; here a double-checked lazy init under a `SemaphoreSlim` guards a name that FirebaseAdmin lets be created only once.
- **Walkthrough**: fields `_gate` (a `SemaphoreSlim(1, 1)`), `_app`, `_messaging` (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:19-21`); `AppName` is `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Fcm.{Guid:N}`, unique per instance (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:24`). `SendEachAsync` (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:27-34`) maps each batch response to `FcmSendOutcome.Success` or an outcome carrying `MessagingErrorCode` and the exception (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:31-33`). `SendEachAsync` is the only messaging call. `GetMessagingAsync` (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:45-69`) returns the cached instance, otherwise waits on the gate, re-checks, fetches `GoogleCredential.GetApplicationDefaultAsync`, and calls `FirebaseApp.Create(..., AppName)` and `FirebaseMessaging.GetMessaging` (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:58-60`). `Dispose` deletes the app and disposes the gate (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:37-43`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the gate exists because two first callers must not race to create the same app name (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:52`). The registration doc comment records why each instance owns a uniquely named app: FirebaseAdmin's app registry is process-global, so the registration creates its own app on first use and deletes it with the service provider, leaving a host's own default app unaffected (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:142-144`). Deferring credential lookup to first use keeps registration free of a network call.
- **Where it's used**: created by `FcmPushClientFactory.CreateMessaging` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmPushClientFactory.cs:41-46`), see [`FcmPushClientFactory`](#fcmpushclientfactory); exercised in `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Notifications/AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPushTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the fast path reads `_messaging` without the gate (`FirebaseMessagingClient.cs:47-50`); a call racing `Dispose` is not guarded, and whether that can happen in practice depends on container shutdown order, which is not determinable from this file.

### FirestoreInstallationRegistry
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:16` · Level 2 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the Firestore implementation of [`IFcmInstallationRegistry`](#ifcminstallationregistry): one document per installation id in the configured collection, with the ownership rules enforced inside Firestore transactions.
- **Depends on**: [`IFcmInstallationRegistry`](#ifcminstallationregistry), [`FcmInstallation`](#fcminstallation), [`FcmNativePushSettings`](#fcmnativepushsettings); Google `FirestoreDb`, `CollectionReference`, `DocumentSnapshot`, `FieldValue`.
- **Concept**: ownership rules as a read-decide-write transaction. The AWS sibling [`SnsInstallationRegistry`](#snsinstallationregistry) expresses the same rule as a DynamoDB condition expression; Firestore has no conditional put, so the rule runs inside `RunTransactionAsync`, which retries on contention so two writers cannot both win. `[Rubric §8, Data Architecture]` assesses consistency under concurrency; `[Rubric §11, Security]` applies because a user cannot take over another device's installation id.
- **Walkthrough**: `MaxUsersPerQuery = 30` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:23`), Firestore's limit on one `WhereIn` filter; `UserId` equality is served by the automatic single-field index, so no composite index is needed (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:19-22`). Field names `UserId`, `Platform`, `PushChannel`, `UpdatedAt` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:25-28`); `Collection` resolves from the options on each access (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:30`). `GetAsync` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:33-37`) reads one snapshot. `TryClaimAsync` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:40-66`) reads the document inside the transaction, returns `(false, existing.PushChannel)` when it exists with another owner and another channel (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:49-54`), else sets all four fields with a server timestamp (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:56-62`) and returns `(true, previous channel)` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:63`). `QueryByUsersAsync` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:69-87`) chunks keys by 30 and runs one `WhereIn` query per chunk (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:74-76`). `DeleteOwnedAsync` and `DeleteIfPushChannelAsync` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:90-95`) both go through `DeleteWhenAsync` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:116-133`), a transaction that deletes only when the document exists and the condition still holds (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:123-129`). `FromSnapshot` (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:97-114`) returns `null` for a missing document or one lacking a non-empty `UserId` or `PushChannel`, and defaults a missing `Platform` to empty (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:112`).
- **Why it's built this way**: an FCM token carries no owner, so the registry is the only place ownership can live (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:110-115`). The conditional deletes make cleanup safe against interleaving: a document that changed hands or tokens since it was read is no longer this delete's to make (`FirestoreInstallationRegistry.cs:125`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered as the singleton `IFcmInstallationRegistry` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:165`); its `MaxUsersPerQuery` constant is also read by [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSender.cs:94`); covered against the emulator by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Emulator.Tests/FcmPushFirestoreEmulatorTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the Firestore database (native mode, the project's default database) is provisioned outside the application (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:138-139`); nothing here creates or validates the collection.

### FcmPushClientFactory
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmPushClientFactory.cs:11` · Level 3 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: builds the `FirestoreDb` and the [`FirebaseMessagingClient`](#firebasemessagingclient) the FCM push adapter needs, from one [`FcmNativePushSettings`](#fcmnativepushsettings).
- **Depends on**: [`FcmNativePushSettings`](#fcmnativepushsettings), [`FirebaseMessagingClient`](#firebasemessagingclient); Google `FirestoreDbBuilder`, `EmulatorDetection`.
- **Concept**: the same factory shape as [`SnsPushClientFactory`](#snspushclientfactory) and [`GcsStorageClientFactory`](#gcsstorageclientfactory): pure functions from settings to clients, so registration code stays declarative and tests can inspect the builder.
- **Walkthrough**: `BuildFirestoreBuilder(settings)` (`FcmPushClientFactory.cs:22-31`) returns a builder for the configured project with `EmulatorDetection = EmulatorDetection.None` (`FcmPushClientFactory.cs:29`). `CreateFirestore` (`FcmPushClientFactory.cs:36`) builds it. `CreateMessaging` (`FcmPushClientFactory.cs:41-46`) returns `new FirebaseMessagingClient(settings.ProjectId!)`.
- **Why it's built this way**: ignoring `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` makes the endpoint a matter of configuration alone, the same posture as the Cloud Storage adapter; an emulator run registers its own `FirestoreDb` first (`FcmPushClientFactory.cs:13-19`).
- **Where it's used**: the `TryAddSingleton` factories in `AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:163-164`), so a host-registered `FirestoreDb` or `IFcmMessagingClient` wins; asserted by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Notifications/AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPushTests.cs`.

### FcmPushDeviceRegistrar
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:23` · Level 4 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the FCM implementation of [`IPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar): each installation is one Firestore document, and that document is the whole registration, because a send resolves the user's registration tokens from these documents (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:10-14`).
- **Depends on**: [`IPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar), [`DeviceInstallationRequest`](group-10-notifications.md#deviceinstallationrequest), [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), [`Error`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error), [`IFcmInstallationRegistry`](#ifcminstallationregistry), [`FcmInstallation`](#fcminstallation); `Grpc.Core.RpcException`.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`FirestoreInstallationRegistry`](#firestoreinstallationregistry) for the ownership rule and [`SnsPushDeviceRegistrar`](#snspushdeviceregistrar) for the AWS twin; the doc comment names the same handover and owner-scoped delete rules as the Notification Hubs and SNS registrars, decided inside a Firestore transaction (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:11-13`). Errors come back as [`Result`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) failures, not exceptions. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses behavior under partial failure and concurrency; here every write is a single conditional registry operation, so a failure leaves either the old document or the new one and never a half-made registration.
- **Walkthrough**: the primary constructor takes only the registry and a logger (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:23-25`). `UpsertAsync` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:28-70`) upper-cases the platform (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:32`); `APNS` gets a `PushDevice.UnsupportedPlatform` validation failure explaining that iOS clients must register an FCM token (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:33-39`), and anything other than `FCMV1` gets the same code (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:41-47`). It builds the installation with the normalized `FcmV1Platform` and the owner's `UserKey` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:49`), calls `TryClaimAsync` keeping only `Claimed` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:54`), and returns success or a `PushDevice.InstallationConflict` conflict (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:55-60`); an `RpcException` maps to `PushDevice.UpsertFailed` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:62-68`). `DeleteAsync` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:73-95`) returns success for an unknown or foreign id (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:78-84`), then deletes conditionally on the owner (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:87`). `DeleteAllForUserAsync` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:102-120`) lists the owner's installations with `QueryByUsersAsync` and deletes each with `DeleteOwnedAsync` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:107-111`). Both delete paths map an `RpcException` to `PushDevice.DeleteFailed` through `DeleteFailed()` (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:90-93,115-118,122-126`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the ownership decision is the whole registration, so another device's installation stays exactly as it is when the claim fails (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:52-53`). The owner-conditional deletes spare a document handed over in the meantime (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:86,98-100`). An unknown or foreign id is reported as success either way; the comment points to the interface remarks on the existence oracle (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:81-82`). Only FCM tokens are accepted: FCM delivers to iOS too, so an iOS client registers the FCM token its Firebase SDK hands out, and a raw APNs token is refused since this adapter has no direct APNs sender (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:16-18`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered transient as `IPushDeviceRegistrar` by `AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:167`), replacing [`NullPushDeviceRegistrar`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#nullpushdeviceregistrar); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Notifications/FcmPushDeviceRegistrarTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: only `RpcException` is caught (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:62,90,115`); any other exception from the registry propagates to the caller. `DeleteAllForUserAsync` stops at the first registry call that throws `RpcException` and returns `DeleteFailed`, leaving the remaining documents for a retry; a `false` from `DeleteOwnedAsync` is not treated as a failure (`FcmPushDeviceRegistrar.cs:110`).

### FcmPushMessage
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmPushMessage.cs:16` · Level 6 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: builds the FirebaseAdmin `Message` for one registration token, carrying both a cross-platform notification and an APNs block.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; FirebaseAdmin `Message`, `Notification`, `ApnsConfig`, `Aps`, `ApsAlert`.
- **Concept**: one message serves Android and iOS. `[Rubric §2, Design Patterns]` assesses whether shared construction is centralized; the payload shape lives in one private `Build`, so one notification looks the same on a device whichever cloud delivered it (`FcmPushMessage.cs:13-14`). Unlike [`SnsPushMessage`](#snspushmessage), it does not call the core `NativePushPayloads` builders, because FirebaseAdmin takes a typed `Message` rather than raw JSON; it maps the same fields instead, and the unit tests pin the equivalence (`FcmPushMessage.cs:7-9`).
- **Walkthrough**: `ApsKey = "aps"` (`FcmPushMessage.cs:19`). `ForToken` (`FcmPushMessage.cs:27-39`) builds and sets `message.Token` inside a `CS0618` suppression (`FcmPushMessage.cs:35-37`). `Build` (`FcmPushMessage.cs:41-61`) null-checks title and body, sets `Notification` (`FcmPushMessage.cs:49`), copies metadata into `Data` only when non-empty (`FcmPushMessage.cs:50`), and sets an `ApnsConfig` with an `ApsAlert` plus the metadata as `CustomData` minus any `aps` key (`FcmPushMessage.cs:51-59`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the obsolete `Message.Token` is kept deliberately: the adapter registers FCM registration tokens (the token-only decision for iOS and Android alike) and the HTTP v1 API still accepts a token target; the comment names the exit, moving registration and send to Firebase installation ids together and dropping the suppression (`FcmPushMessage.cs:31-34`). Filtering `aps` out of `CustomData` keeps metadata from clobbering the alert block (`FcmPushMessage.cs:18`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: [`FcmNativePushSender`](#fcmnativepushsender), one message per distinct token (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSender.cs:50`); asserted in `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Notifications/FcmNativePushSenderTests.cs`.

### FcmNativePushSender
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Notifications` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/FcmNativePushSender.cs:29` · Level 7 · class (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: the FCM implementation of [`INativePushSender`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inativepushsender): a user-targeted send resolves the users' registration tokens through the Firestore registry and sends one message per distinct token in batches, returning a [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome) (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:9-13,35`).
- **Depends on**: [`INativePushSender`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inativepushsender), [`NativePushOutcome`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushoutcome), [`NativePushDeliveryUnit`](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#nativepushdeliveryunit), [`IFcmInstallationRegistry`](#ifcminstallationregistry), [`IFcmMessagingClient`](#ifcmmessagingclient), [`FcmInstallation`](#fcminstallation), [`FcmPushMessage`](#fcmpushmessage), [`FcmSendOutcome`](#fcmsendoutcome), [`FirestoreInstallationRegistry`](#firestoreinstallationregistry) (for its `MaxUsersPerQuery` constant), [`FirstFailure`](#firstfailure) (nested); FirebaseAdmin `FirebaseException`, `Grpc.Core.RpcException`, `LoggerMessage` source generation.
- **Concept**: cross-reference [`SnsNativePushSender`](#snsnativepushsender) for the AWS twin. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses partial-failure behavior: a lookup failure skips only that batch of users, a failed send call skips only that chunk, and a failed token skips only that token, while the first failure is still reported in the outcome. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` applies too: sends go out in batches of up to 500 rather than one HTTP call per device.
- **Walkthrough**: the primary constructor takes the registry, the messaging client and a logger (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:29-32`). `SendToUsersAsync` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:35-88`) creates a [`FirstFailure`](#firstfailure) (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:39`) and resolves installations through `LookupAsync` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:91-108`), which converts ids with `FcmInstallation.UserKey`, de-duplicates, chunks by `FirestoreInstallationRegistry.MaxUsersPerQuery` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:94`), and logs, records and skips a batch whose query throws `RpcException` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:100-104`). It groups installations by token so two ids holding one token are one device (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:42-45`), then sends chunks of `IFcmMessagingClient.MaxMessagesPerSend` built with `FcmPushMessage.ForToken` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:48-54`), logging, recording and continuing on `FirebaseException` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:56-61`). Per outcome it counts a success, records and prunes on `IsStaleToken`, or records and logs the error code (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:63-80`), then logs the delivered count (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:83`) and returns `NativePushOutcome { Unit = NativePushDeliveryUnit.Message, FirstFailure = ... }` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:87`). `PruneAsync` (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:110-124`) deletes every installation in the group with the token-conditional `DeleteIfPushChannelAsync`, logging an `RpcException`. Six source-generated log methods follow (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:126-142`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the outcome carries the first failure but no per-platform counts, because an installation records platform `fcmv1` on Android and iOS alike, so a token does not say which phone it reached; a dead token counts as a failure since its device was not reached (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:20-24,85-86`). The token-conditional prune never removes an installation refreshed onto a new token in the meantime (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Notifications/IFcmInstallationRegistry.cs:44-47`). See [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered transient as `INativePushSender` by `AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPush` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:166`), replacing [`NullNativePushSender`](group-14-module-system-composition.md#nullnativepushsender); covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Notifications/FcmNativePushSenderTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: a prune failure is logged but not offered to [`FirstFailure`](#firstfailure) (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:119-122`); the stale token itself already was. The batching reads a constant from the concrete [`FirestoreInstallationRegistry`](#firestoreinstallationregistry) rather than from [`IFcmInstallationRegistry`](#ifcminstallationregistry) (`FcmNativePushSender.cs:94`).

### GcsFileStorageSettings
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Storage/GcsFileStorageSettings.cs:12` · Level 0 · class (public sealed)

- **What it is**: the options class bound from the `FileStorage` section for the Google Cloud Storage adapter: the bucket, an optional public URL base, an optional endpoint override and an optional quota project (`GcsFileStorageSettings.cs:12-45`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; BCL `Uri` only.
- **Concept**: one shared `FileStorage` section, vendor-prefixed keys. `SectionName` is `"FileStorage"` (`:15`), the same section [AzureBlobFileStorageSettings](#azureblobfilestoragesettings) and [S3FileStorageSettings](#s3filestoragesettings) bind, but every key here carries a `Gcs` prefix, so a section written for another cloud never accidentally enables this adapter. `[Rubric §33, Developer Experience]` assesses whether configuration is discoverable and hard to misuse: every property is `init`-only and documents its own default and failure mode in its XML comment.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`GcsBucket`** (`:18`): required; the comment states the bucket is provisioned by infrastructure, not created here (`:17`).
  - **`GcsPublicBaseUrl`** (`:26`): an optional CDN or load balancer base; when set, a returned URL is `{GcsPublicBaseUrl}/{object}` without the bucket (`:20-25`).
  - **`GcsServiceUrl`** (`:36`): an optional JSON API root such as the fake-gcs-server emulator. A plain-HTTP endpoint is treated as an emulator and called without credentials, so no bearer token travels in clear; an HTTPS endpoint keeps Application Default Credentials (`:28-35`).
  - **`GcsProjectId`** (`:44`): the optional `x-goog-user-project` quota project, needed only with user-account credentials such as `gcloud auth application-default login` (`:38-43`).
  - Both URI properties state "Only an absolute URL counts" (`:24`, `:34`); the consumers enforce that with `IsAbsoluteUri` patterns (see [GcsObjectUrl](#gcsobjecturl) and [GcsStorageClientFactory](#gcsstorageclientfactory)).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html) records managed file storage, and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html) the vendor-adapter split. There are no key or secret properties at all: authentication is Application Default Credentials only.
- **Where it's used**: bound and gated by [GcpServiceCollectionExtensions](#gcpservicecollectionextensions)`.AddGoogleCloudStorage` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:53-60`), read by [GcsStorageClientFactory](#gcsstorageclientfactory), [GcsObjectUrl](#gcsobjecturl), [GcsFileStorageService](#gcsfilestorageservice) and [GcpDataProtectionBuilderExtensions](#gcpdataprotectionbuilderextensions). Covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Storage/GcsStorageClientFactoryTests.cs` and `GcsObjectUrlTests.cs`.

### IGoogleCloudTelemetryTokenSource
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Telemetry/IGoogleCloudTelemetryTokenSource.cs:8` · Level 0 · interface (internal)

- **What it is**: a one-method internal abstraction that returns a current OAuth2 access token for the Google Cloud telemetry exporter: `Task<string> GetTokenAsync(CancellationToken)` (`IGoogleCloudTelemetryTokenSource.cs:13`).
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party.
- **Concept**: a test boundary around the credential. `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether external dependencies can be substituted in tests: the production implementation calls Google's credential APIs, and this interface lets the test project substitute `FakeTelemetryTokenSource` (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Telemetry/FakeTelemetryTokenSource.cs`) without any Google dependency.
- **Walkthrough**: the single member's cancellation token cancels both the credential load and the token request (`:11`).
- **Why it's built this way**: registered with `TryAddSingleton` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:129`), so a test registers its fake first and the default does not replace it.
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [ApplicationDefaultTelemetryTokenSource](#applicationdefaulttelemetrytokensource), consumed by [GoogleCloudTelemetryAccessToken](#googlecloudtelemetryaccesstoken), registered by [GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions](#googlecloudtelemetryexporterextensions).

### ApplicationDefaultTelemetryTokenSource
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Telemetry/ApplicationDefaultTelemetryTokenSource.cs:11` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: the production [IGoogleCloudTelemetryTokenSource](#igooglecloudtelemetrytokensource): it loads Google Application Default Credentials once, scopes them, and asks them for an access token on each call (`ApplicationDefaultTelemetryTokenSource.cs:11-38`).
- **Depends on**: [IGoogleCloudTelemetryTokenSource](#igooglecloudtelemetrytokensource); externals `GoogleCredential` and `ITokenAccess` from Google.Apis.Auth.
- **Concept**: lazy, retry-friendly credential load. `[Rubric §11, Security]` assesses least privilege: the requested scope is the broad `cloud-platform` (`:19`), and the comment records why: the narrower `trace.append` does not cover the logs this exporter also writes, so the effective bound is the identity's IAM roles (`roles/telemetry.writer`, `roles/serviceusage.serviceUsageConsumer`) rather than the scope (`:13-18`).
- **Walkthrough**: `Scope` constant (`:19`); a nullable `_credential` cache (`:21`). `GetTokenAsync` (`:24-37`) loads `GoogleCredential.GetApplicationDefaultAsync` and `CreateScoped(Scope)` only when the cache is empty (`:29-34`), then returns `GetAccessTokenForRequestAsync(authUri: null, ...)` (`:36`). The comment states a race between two first callers loads twice and keeps the last (harmless), and a failed load is not cached, so the next refresh retries (`:26-27`).
- **Why it's built this way**: nothing touches Google at registration; the credential is first loaded by the hosted refresher at host start (see [GoogleCloudTelemetryAccessToken](#googlecloudtelemetryaccesstoken)), which keeps a host without credentials startable. Telemetry rationale: [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered as the default token source by [GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions](#googlecloudtelemetryexporterextensions) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:129`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: whether `GetAccessTokenForRequestAsync` serves a cached token or fetches a new one per call is decided inside Google.Apis.Auth, not in this file.

### GcsObjectUrl
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Storage/GcsObjectUrl.cs:9` · Level 1 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: the pure function that composes the absolute URL an upload returns for one object (`GcsObjectUrl.cs:28`).
- **Depends on**: [GcsFileStorageSettings](#gcsfilestoragesettings); BCL `Uri`.
- **Concept**: the same split as [S3ObjectUrl](#s3objecturl): URL composition lives outside the service so it is unit-testable with no client. `[Rubric §14, Testability]` applies directly, covered by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Storage/GcsObjectUrlTests.cs`.
- **Walkthrough**: `PublicEndpoint` is `https://storage.googleapis.com/` (`:12`). `Compose` (`:28-42`) null-guards both arguments, escapes the name, then picks in order (`:15-21`): an absolute `GcsPublicBaseUrl` yields `{base}/{object}` with no bucket (`:35-38`); otherwise an absolute `GcsServiceUrl` or the public endpoint yields `{endpoint}/{bucket}/{object}` (`:40-41`). `EscapeName` splits on `/`, applies `Uri.EscapeDataString` to each segment and rejoins (`:44-45`), so spaces and non-ASCII names still produce a valid URL while keeping the separators. `TrimEnd` strips a trailing slash from the base (`:47`).
- **Why it's built this way**: a CDN or load balancer domain already points at the bucket, so repeating the bucket would break the URL (`:17-18`).
- **Where it's used**: [GcsFileStorageService](#gcsfilestorageservice)`.UploadAsync` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Storage/GcsFileStorageService.cs:48`).

### GcsStorageClientFactory
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Storage/GcsStorageClientFactory.cs:11` · Level 1 · class (internal static)

- **What it is**: maps [GcsFileStorageSettings](#gcsfilestoragesettings) onto Google's `StorageClientBuilder` and builds the `StorageClient` (`GcsStorageClientFactory.cs:23`, `:46`).
- **Depends on**: [GcsFileStorageSettings](#gcsfilestoragesettings); externals `StorageClientBuilder`, `StorageClient` (Google.Cloud.Storage.V1).
- **Concept**: the builder/build split, the same shape as [S3StorageClientFactory](#s3storageclientfactory). `BuildBuilder` returns the unbuilt builder so tests assert the mapping without network access (`MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Storage/GcsStorageClientFactoryTests.cs`). `[Rubric §11, Security]` applies to the emulator rule: credentials are dropped only for a plain-HTTP endpoint, so a bearer token never crosses an unencrypted connection.
- **Walkthrough**: `BuildBuilder` (`:23-41`) null-guards the settings and starts from a default builder (Google endpoint, Application Default Credentials). An absolute `GcsServiceUrl` sets `BaseUri` to `{GcsServiceUrl}/storage/v1/` (`:31`) and sets `UnauthenticatedAccess` when the scheme is `http` (`:32`). A non-blank `GcsProjectId` sets `QuotaProject` (`:35-38`). `Create` is `BuildBuilder(settings).Build()` (`:46`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the doc comment states `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` is ignored (the builder default), so the endpoint is decided by configuration alone (`:18-19`).
- **Where it's used**: [GcpServiceCollectionExtensions](#gcpservicecollectionextensions)`.AddGoogleCloudStorage` (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:62`) and [GcpDataProtectionBuilderExtensions](#gcpdataprotectionbuilderextensions).

### GoogleCloudDataProtectionExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleCloudDataProtectionExtensions.cs:22` · Level 1 · class (static, `extension<TBuilder>`)

- **What it is**: `AddGoogleCloudDataProtection()`, which persists the DataProtection key ring to one Google Cloud Storage object and optionally encrypts each key at rest with a Cloud KMS key (`GoogleCloudDataProtectionExtensions.cs:31-33`).
- **Depends on**: [GcsObjectLocation](#gcsobjectlocation), [GcpDataProtectionBuilderExtensions](#gcpdataprotectionbuilderextensions) (`PersistKeysToGoogleCloudStorage`, `ProtectKeysWithGoogleCloudKms`), [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames), [GcpKeyRingAtRestNoticeService](#gcpkeyringatrestnoticeservice) (owner of the `KmsKeyNameKey` constant and the missing-key Warning); externals `CryptoKeyName` (Google.Cloud.Kms.V1), `AddDataProtection`.
- **Concept**: the gated host integration taught at [AwsDataProtectionExtensions](#awsdataprotectionextensions), with validation before registration. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]`: the object is one document rewritten under a generation precondition, so two replicas creating a key at once cannot overwrite each other; the loser re-reads and retries (`:52-54`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Keys** (`:24-25`, `:37-48`): `DataProtection:GcsObjectUri` (the gate, `gs://bucket/path/keys.xml`), `DataProtection:GcpKmsKeyName` (optional full crypto key name; the constant is `GcpKeyRingAtRestNoticeService.KmsKeyNameKey`, `:25`), `DataProtection:ApplicationName` (optional).
  - **Gate** (`:79-88`): blank returns with no receipt, so an object URI that appears later still trips the startup check (`:81-84`).
  - **Validation** (`:90-99`): `GcsObjectLocation.Parse` and `CryptoKeyName.TryParse` both fail with the offending key named before anything is registered.
  - **Registration** (`:101-123`): receipt (`:103`), application name, `PersistKeysToGoogleCloudStorage(location)` (`:110`), then `ProtectKeysWithGoogleCloudKms` only when a key name parsed (`:112-115`). With no key name outside Development it registers `GcpKeyRingAtRestNoticeService` as a hosted service instead (`:116-121`), so an unencrypted key ring is a single startup Warning naming the missing key, not a failure (`:44-45`).
- **Why it's built this way**: encryption is a separate optional step "for the reason the Azure sibling gives" (`:64-65`, see [AzureDataProtectionExtensions](#azuredataprotectionextensions)); the missing-key notice follows the same reasoning (`:118-119`). A host keeps exactly one key ring: setting this object together with `DataProtection:BlobStorageUri` or `DataProtection:S3ObjectUri` fails the startup tripwire (`:67-69`). IAM needs (`storage.objects.get/create/delete`, `roles/cloudkms.cryptoKeyEncrypterDecrypter`) are stated at `:58-61`. Recorded in [ADR-069](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/069-shared-data-protection-key-ring.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: no call site in `MMCA.ADC/Source` or elsewhere in `MMCA.Common/Source`; the tripwire message naming it is at `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:136`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the `StorageClient` reuse described at `:54-57` (a host-registered client, else one built over Application Default Credentials) lives in [GcpDataProtectionBuilderExtensions](#gcpdataprotectionbuilderextensions), not in this file.

### GoogleCloudTelemetryAccessToken
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Telemetry/GoogleCloudTelemetryAccessToken.cs:28` · Level 1 · class (internal sealed partial)

- **What it is**: an `IHostedService` that holds the current Google access token for the telemetry exporter and refreshes it in the background (`GoogleCloudTelemetryAccessToken.cs:28-30`).
- **Depends on**: [IGoogleCloudTelemetryTokenSource](#igooglecloudtelemetrytokensource); externals `IHostedService`, `ILogger`, OpenTelemetry `SuppressInstrumentationScope`.
- **Concept**: a background-refreshed credential so the HTTP send path never awaits a token. `[Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]` assesses whether a dependency failure degrades rather than takes down the host: a failed refresh keeps the last good token and is logged, never thrown, because "telemetry export is not worth failing the host over" (`:65-67`). `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the refresh runs inside `SuppressInstrumentationScope.Begin()` (`:75`), so the credential's own metadata-server and token-endpoint calls are not traced and exported once a minute as noise (`:73-74`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Intervals** (`:33`, `:36`): `RefreshInterval` 1 minute, `FailureRetryInterval` 30 seconds.
  - **State** (`:38-43`): a stopping `CancellationTokenSource`, a `volatile string? _current` exposed as `Current`, and the loop task.
  - **`StartAsync`** (`:46-50`): one awaited refresh, then the loop is started with that result.
  - **`RefreshAsync`** (`:71-92`): reads the token; rethrows only a requested cancellation (`:81-84`), otherwise logs `LogRefreshFailed` and returns `false` (`:85-91`). The catch-all is a deliberate `CA1031` suppression scoped to that one clause, justified as "logged and retried on the failure interval" (`:85`, `:87`).
  - **`RefreshLoopAsync`** (`:94-108`): delays by the success or failure interval, refreshes, repeats until stopped.
  - **`StopAsync` / `Dispose`** (`:53-60`, `:63`): cancel, await the loop with the caller's token, dispose the token source.
  - **Log** (`:110-111`): a source-generated `Warning` stating traces and logs are not exported until a refresh succeeds.
- **Why it's built this way**: the export path reads a held string instead of awaiting a credential, which also serves the synchronous `Send` override in [GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler](#googlecloudtelemetryauthhandler). Telemetry rationale: [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html).
- **Where it's used**: registered as a singleton and as a hosted service by [GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions](#googlecloudtelemetryexporterextensions) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:130-131`), read by [GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler](#googlecloudtelemetryauthhandler). Exercised by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Telemetry/GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandlerTests.cs`.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: `StartAsync` awaits the first refresh, so how long host start waits on an unreachable credential endpoint depends on Google.Apis.Auth's own timeouts, which are not visible here.

### GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Telemetry` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Telemetry/GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler.cs:14` · Level 2 · class (internal sealed)

- **What it is**: a `DelegatingHandler` that stamps the bearer token and the `x-goog-user-project` quota-project header on every OTLP export request (`GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler.cs:14-18`).
- **Depends on**: [GoogleCloudTelemetryAccessToken](#googlecloudtelemetryaccesstoken); BCL `DelegatingHandler`, `AuthenticationHeaderValue`.
- **Concept**: fail closed on a missing credential. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: with no token yet, the handler throws `HttpRequestException` with `HttpStatusCode.ServiceUnavailable` (`:46-52`), which the OTLP exporter records as a failed export, so no request without a credential ever leaves the process (`:36-40`).
- **Walkthrough**: `QuotaProjectHeader` constant (`:18`). Both `Send` (`:22-26`) and `SendAsync` (`:30-34`) call `Authorize` and delegate. `Authorize` (`:41-57`) reads `accessToken.Current`, sets `Authorization: Bearer {token}` (`:54`), and replaces any existing quota-project header with the configured project (`:55-56`).
- **Why it's built this way**: overriding the synchronous `Send` too covers OTLP exporter paths that send synchronously; both paths share one `Authorize`. Telemetry rationale: [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html).
- **Where it's used**: constructed per exporter `HttpClient` in [GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions](#googlecloudtelemetryexporterextensions) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:203-207`). Tested in `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Telemetry/GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandlerTests.cs`.

---

### GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensions.cs:23` · Level 2 · class (static, `extension<TBuilder>`)

- **What it is**: `AddGoogleSecretManagerConfiguration()`, which adds a Google Cloud Secret Manager configuration source so every secret under one id prefix reads through `IConfiguration` (`GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationExtensions.cs:31-33`).
- **Depends on**: [GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource](#googlesecretmanagerconfigurationsource), [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames); external `SecretManagerServiceClient`.
- **Concept**: the secrets-as-configuration convention taught at [AwsSecretsManagerConfigurationProvider](#awssecretsmanagerconfigurationprovider), on Google. `[Rubric §33, Developer Experience]`: the naming is the Key Vault one, so a secret set moves between clouds by renaming its prefix alone: `adc-prod-ConnectionStrings--Default` arrives as `ConnectionStrings:Default` (`:48-55`). The `latest` version is read; a disabled, destroyed or version-less secret is skipped (`:53-55`).
- **Walkthrough**: `SectionPath` is `"GoogleSecretManager"` (`:25`). Keys (`:37-44`): `ProjectId` and `NamePrefix` together are the gate, `ReloadIntervalMinutes` optional. An incomplete gate returns with no receipt (`:88-95`). The reload interval is parsed and a bad value throws (`:100-112`), as in [AzureKeyVaultConfigurationExtensions](#azurekeyvaultconfigurationextensions). Then the receipt (`:116`) and `configuration.Add(new GoogleSecretManagerConfigurationSource(SecretManagerServiceClient.Create, projectId, namePrefix, reloadInterval))` (`:118-122`).
- **Why it's built this way**: not called from `AddServiceDefaults()` for the Key Vault reason, and read synchronously at the call, so it belongs early in the host builder (`:67-73`). Required IAM (`secretmanager.secrets.list`, `secretmanager.versions.get`, `secretmanager.versions.access`) is stated at `:58-64`; a secret the identity cannot read fails startup, naming it (`:64`). Recorded in [ADR-061](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/061-runtime-secret-management.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: no call site in `MMCA.ADC/Source`; the tripwire message naming it is at `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:131`.

### GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:20` · Level 3 · class (static, `extension<TBuilder>`)

- **What it is**: `AddGoogleCloudTelemetryExporter()`, which exports traces and logs (not metrics) to the Google Cloud Telemetry API over OTLP HTTP/protobuf when `GoogleCloudTelemetry:ProjectId` is set, landing them in Cloud Trace and Cloud Logging (`GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensions.cs:44-47`).
- **Depends on**: [IGoogleCloudTelemetryTokenSource](#igooglecloudtelemetrytokensource), [ApplicationDefaultTelemetryTokenSource](#applicationdefaulttelemetrytokensource), [GoogleCloudTelemetryAccessToken](#googlecloudtelemetryaccesstoken), [GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler](#googlecloudtelemetryauthhandler), [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames); externals `OtlpTraceExporter`, `OtlpLogExporter`, `BatchActivityExportProcessor`, `BatchLogRecordExportProcessor`, `OtlpExporterOptions`.
- **Concept**: a second vendor exporter beside the vendor-neutral one. `[Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]`: the same ordering precondition as [AzureMonitorExporterExtensions](#azuremonitorexporterextensions) is enforced (`:103-111`). `[Rubric §31, Cost/FinOps]` and §13 together explain why metrics are excluded: the Telemetry API takes at most 200 points per metric request and the .NET SDK cannot split one metric across requests, so metrics go through a Google-built Collector with `send_batch_max_size: 200` instead (`:78-86`).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **Constants** (`:23-38`): `ProjectIdKey`, `EndpointKey`, `DefaultEndpoint` (`https://telemetry.googleapis.com/`), and `ProjectIdAttribute` = `gcp.project_id`, the resource attribute Google maps data to a project with (`:31-37`).
  - **Gate** (`:113-120`): a blank project id registers nothing, no receipt.
  - **Registration** (`:122-131`): trims the id, resolves the endpoint, registers the receipt, `TryAddSingleton` of the token source, the access-token singleton and its hosted-service registration.
  - **Pipeline** (`:137-144`): adds the `gcp.project_id` resource attribute and hand-built batch processors for `v1/traces` and `v1/logs`. The comment at `:133-136` explains why not `AddOtlpExporter`: the SDK refuses a signal-specific `AddOtlpExporter` where `UseOtlpExporter` (enabled by service defaults) also runs.
  - **`ResolveEndpoint`** (`:157-175`): default when blank, throws on a non-absolute or non-http(s) value (`:165-169`), and appends a trailing slash (tested with a span `EndsWith('/')` on `AbsolutePath`) so `new Uri(root, "v1/traces")` does not replace the last segment (`:171-174`). `SignalEndpoint` is that `Uri` combine (`:181`).
  - **`CreateExporterOptions`** (`:193-209`): full endpoint URL, `HttpProtobuf`, `Headers = null` (`:200`) so an `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_HEADERS` meant for the vendor-neutral exporter never reaches Google (`:188-191`), and an `HttpClientFactory` wrapping [GoogleCloudTelemetryAuthHandler](#googlecloudtelemetryauthhandler) with the exporter's timeout (`:203-207`).
- **Why it's built this way**: credentials load at host start, not at registration, and a host without them still starts with exports refused locally (`:62-67`). Resource mapping (`generic_task` versus `generic_node`) is left to `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME` and `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` (`:70-75`). Recorded in [ADR-041](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/041-observability-and-telemetry.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: no call site in `MMCA.ADC/Source`; tripwire message at `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:141`. Tested by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Telemetry/GoogleCloudTelemetryExporterExtensionsTests.cs` and `GoogleCloudTelemetryExportTests.cs`.

---

### GcsFileStorageService
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp.Storage` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/Storage/GcsFileStorageService.cs:25` · Level 4 · class (public sealed)

- **What it is**: the Google Cloud Storage implementation of [IFileStorageService](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ifilestorageservice): upload returns the object URL, delete is delete-if-exists (`GcsFileStorageService.cs:25-28`).
- **Depends on**: [IFileStorageService](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ifilestorageservice), [FileUploadOptions](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#fileuploadoptions), [GcsFileStorageSettings](#gcsfilestoragesettings), [GcsObjectUrl](#gcsobjecturl), [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result), [Error](group-01-result-error-handling.md#error); externals `StorageClient`, `StorageObject`, `GoogleApiException`.
- **Concept**: one port, three vendor adapters with matching error contracts, as with [AzureBlobFileStorageService](#azureblobfilestorageservice) and [S3FileStorageService](#s3filestorageservice). `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` (dependency inversion): callers depend on the core interface and never see Google types. Failures map to `Result` with stable codes `FileStorage.UploadFailed` / `FileStorage.DeleteFailed`, not exceptions (see the [Result](group-01-result-error-handling.md#result) pattern).
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`IsConfigured`** is `true` (`:33`). The short `UploadAsync` overload forwards with `FileUploadOptions.None` (`:37-38`).
  - **`UploadAsync`** (`:42-84`): composes the URL first so a configuration that cannot name the object leaves no orphan (`:46-48`); builds a `StorageObject` whose `ContentDisposition` and `CacheControl` are set only when non-empty, stored as object metadata (`:50-59`); uploads without closing the caller's stream (`:63-65`). `GoogleApiException` and transport failures log and return `UploadFailed` (`:68-83`).
  - **`DeleteAsync`** (`:87-116`): a `404` `GoogleApiException` is success (`:94-99`), mirroring `DeleteBlobIfExists`; other API and transport failures return `DeleteFailed`.
  - **`IsTransportFailure`** (`:124-126`): `HttpRequestException`, `IOException`, or an `OperationCanceledException` the caller did not request; the caller's own cancellation still propagates.
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html) defines the storage port; the RS0026 suppressions document that the overload pair mirrors the grandfathered interface: the short overload's justification records that both overloads entered this package's public API as a pair, as on the Azure and S3 siblings (`:36`), and the long one carries the grandfathered-baseline justification (`:41`).
- **Where it's used**: registered by [GcpServiceCollectionExtensions](#gcpservicecollectionextensions) (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:63`). Tested by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/Storage/GcsFileStorageServiceTests.cs`; also referenced by `CloudVendorRulesTests.cs` in the same project.

### GcpServiceCollectionExtensions
> MMCA.Common.Gcp · `MMCA.Common.Gcp` · `MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp/GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:27` · Level 8 · class (static, `extension(IServiceCollection)`)

- **What it is**: the GCP adapter's DI entry points: `AddGoogleCloudStorage`, `AddCloudSqlIamAuthentication`, and `AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPush` (`GcpServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:49`, `:91`, `:149`).
- **Depends on**: [GcsFileStorageSettings](#gcsfilestoragesettings), [GcsStorageClientFactory](#gcsstorageclientfactory), [GcsFileStorageService](#gcsfilestorageservice), [CloudSqlIamAuthenticationSettings](#cloudsqliamauthenticationsettings), [CloudSqlIamAuthenticationConfigurator](#cloudsqliamauthenticationconfigurator), [FcmNativePushSettings](#fcmnativepushsettings), [FcmPushClientFactory](#fcmpushclientfactory), [IFcmMessagingClient](#ifcmmessagingclient), [IFcmInstallationRegistry](#ifcminstallationregistry), [FirestoreInstallationRegistry](#firestoreinstallationregistry), [FcmNativePushSender](#fcmnativepushsender), [FcmPushDeviceRegistrar](#fcmpushdeviceregistrar), [IFileStorageService](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ifilestorageservice), [INativePushSender](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inativepushsender), [IPushDeviceRegistrar](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#ipushdeviceregistrar), [INpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator](group-07-persistence-ef-core.md#inpgsqldatasourceconfigurator), [HostIntegrationMarker](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationmarker), [HostIntegrationNames](group-16-aspire-orchestration.md#hostintegrationnames).
- **Concept**: the same gated, unconditionally-callable shape as [AzureServiceCollectionExtensions](#azureservicecollectionextensions) and [AwsServiceCollectionExtensions](#awsservicecollectionextensions), with fail-fast validation at registration. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: every GCP path authenticates with Application Default Credentials, with no key-file or HMAC setting (`:38-39`, `:133-135`), and Cloud SQL IAM auth refuses connection strings that would send the token in clear and leaves a startup-tripwire receipt so the gate cannot be set without the call.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - **`AddGoogleCloudStorage`** (`:49-66`): binds `FileStorage` (`:53-54`) and returns when `GcsBucket` is blank (`:56-60`), even if the section carries the S3 `Bucket` or Azure `ContainerName` (`:39-41`). Registers the client through `TryAddSingleton(_ => GcsStorageClientFactory.Create(settings))` (`:62`), so a host-registered `StorageClient` wins, and the transient service (`:63`).
  - **`AddCloudSqlIamAuthentication`** (`:91-117`): gated on `CloudSql:IamAuthentication:Enabled` (`:95-99`); calls `EnsureConfiguredConnectionStringsAreEncrypted` so a non-TLS PostgreSQL connection string fails at registration (`:101-103`). Past the gate it adds one `HostIntegrationMarker(HostIntegrationNames.GoogleCloudSqlIamAuthentication)` receipt, skipped when one is already registered (`:105-114`): with the gate set and no receipt the core startup tripwire fails the host instead of letting it log in with whatever password the connection string carries (`MMCA.Common/Source/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Aspire/Integrations/HostIntegrationTripwire.cs:160-162`). Then `AddNpgsqlDataSourceConfigurator(new CloudSqlIamAuthenticationConfigurator())` (`:116`). The doc comment records the token scope, the 1-minute refresh and 30-second failure retry, no password in the connection string, and the `--auto-iam-authn` proxy alternative (`:69-84`); SQL Server, SQLite and Cosmos are untouched.
  - **`AddFirebaseCloudMessagingPush`** (`:149-170`): requires `NativePush:Enabled` true and a complete `NativePush:Fcm` section, that is `ProjectId` and `InstallationsCollection` (`:131-132`, `:153-160`), then binds options and registers Firestore, the messaging client, the installation registry, the sender and the registrar (`:162-167`). There is no topic setting and no topic validation. The doc comment explains the Firestore installation registry, the `roles/firebasecloudmessaging.admin` (sends) and `roles/datastore.user` roles, and that raw APNs tokens are rejected with `PushDevice.UnsupportedPlatform` (`:120-145`).
- **Why it's built this way**: [ADR-045](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/045-managed-file-storage-and-avatars.html) (storage), [ADR-044](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/044-native-push-delivery.html) (push), [ADR-113](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/113-postgresql-as-a-first-class-engine.html) (PostgreSQL), [ADR-070](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/070-fail-fast-configuration-contract.html) (fail-fast configuration), [ADR-106](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/106-extension-members-as-public-di-surface.html) and [ADR-134](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/134-cloud-neutral-core-vendor-adapters.html).
- **Where it's used**: no call site in `MMCA.ADC/Source`. Tested by `MMCA.Common/Tests/Hosting/MMCA.Common.Gcp.Tests/GcpServiceCollectionExtensionsTests.cs`.
